One who knows this principle himself attains the nature of Aditi; thus the explanation of the name Aditi is given and the universe-governing nature of this deity is stated. For this reason the Supreme Brahman also is designated by this name.
Besides, in "विश्वेषामदितिर्यज्ञयानां (*viśveṣāmaditiryajñayānāṃ*)" (4-1-20) and "अमूरः कविरदितिविस्वान् (*amūraḥ kaviraditivisvān*)" (7-9-3) and similar places the word Aditi is an epithet of Agni. In 9-80-5 it is an epithet of Aryaman **NOTE**. Yāska, in the Nirukta, has stated "अग्निरप्यदितिरुच्यते (*agnirapyaditirucyate*)" (Ni. 10-23), that Agni also is denoted by the word Aditi. And to support his sentence which teaches this identity,

### Printed p. 35
"यस्मै त्वं सुद्रविषो ददाशोऽनागास्त्वमिदिते सर्वताता (*yasmai tvaṃ sudraviṣo dadāśo 'nāgāstvamidite sarvatātā*)"
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-94-15)
having quoted this sentence, he has stated that there the word Aditi is used as an epithet of the word Agni. Sāyaṇa has explained that it means the indivisible, that is, the complete Agni.
Thus all the sentences that give the meaning of seeking the protection of Aditi, the destroyer of sin and forgiver of sins, and asking forgiveness, describe the infinite power of Aditi. These epithets and descriptions show that Aditi is the one who has the power of governing the universe. It is also understood that these ordinances are not merely a display of physical power but include spiritual and ethical laws. In the same way the deities Savitṛ, Sūrya, Uṣas, heaven and earth, and the like are also prayed to (4-54-3; 10-35-2, 3; 3-54-19 and so on).
**1. Some passages describing that Aditi, though governor of the universe, is also controlled by other powers:—** **NOTE**
There are also some descriptions that the deities such as Varuṇa, who are called lords of the universe, and even Aditi, who is described as mother, are at times subject to the control of other powers. For example:—
In the mantra 7-38-4 there is the description that Aditi, together with her sons, the deities Varuṇa, Mitra, Aryaman **NOTE** and the rest, praises Savitṛ and prays for the help of Savitṛ. In 8-12-14 it is stated that Aditi made a work in the form of a hymn of praise to praise Indra. (अदितिः स्तोममिंद्राय जीजनत् (*aditiḥ stomamiṃdrāya jījanat*))
**10. The order of the creation of the universe as described in the 72nd sūkta of the 10th maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā: the nature of the origin of Aditi, Dakṣa, the Ādityas and the other gods:—**
It is the opinion of some scholars that the 72nd sūkta of the 10th maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā is not as ancient as the other sūktas of the Saṃhitā. But no proper basis can be given for this. The examination of the question whether this sūkta is ancient or recent is for the present not to the point; what is useful here is the order of description of the creation of the universe as set out in this sūkta. Its details are as follows.
(1) With these praising sūkta mantras we shall describe the order of the origin of the gods. With the help of these mantras in the form of praise, the gods of the earlier age can know our deeds, which belong to the later age.

### Printed p. 36
(2) Just as a smith presses the bellows and makes the air that is inside come out, Brahmaṇaspati made these gods, who were absorbed, appear outside in the form of creation. In the most ancient age of the gods, the existent came into manifestation from the non-existent.
(3) In the beginning the manifestation of the existent arose from the non-existent. From it the quarters were made manifest. After that, from the trees ............
(4) From the trees the earth, and from the earth the quarters, were born. From Dakṣa Aditi, and from Aditi Dakṣa, were born.
(5) O Dakṣa, Aditi was born of you. She is your daughter. After that, from her the gods, auspicious and immortal, were born.
(6) O gods, when you moved, when you made the waters of the atmosphere surge, then, as dust goes out from the bodies of dancers, so particles of dust came out from their bodies.
(7) O gods, when, like men able to bear toil, you pervaded the universe, adorned it and fashioned it, then you brought out the sun, who was hidden in the ocean in the form of the atmosphere, and made him shine.
(8) Of the eight sons born of her body, Aditi approached the gods with seven only; the eighth, Mārtāṇḍa, she placed in the atmosphere.
(9) Aditi went with seven sons and joined the gods of the former age. The eighth, Mārtāṇḍa, she took again; she gave him birth for his production and for his death.
Here it is pertinent to examine what the word यतयः (*yatayaḥ*) used in the 7th Ṛk means. A relation is stated, in some places, between that creation of the universe which is described in the Ṛk 11-22-3 **NOTE** and the Bhṛgus described in the mantras 8-3-9 and 8-6-18. Botlink **NOTE** and Roth are of opinion that in the present mantra the word यतयः (*yatayaḥ*) designates those Bhṛgus. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-13-1 the same word is an epithet of Agni (वैश्वानराय यतये मतीनां (*vaiśvānarāya yataye matīnāṃ*)). In 8-6-18, in "ये इंद्र यतयस्त्वा भृगवो ये च (*ye iṃdra yatayastvā bhṛgavo ye ca*) ........", यतयः (*yatayaḥ*) may be taken to mean the descendants of the master of a group.

### Printed p. 37
There are some Sāma mantras that are not included in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā. In one of them both the words यति (*yati*) and भृगु (*bhṛgu*) are used. (Sā. Ve. 2-304) **NOTE** "इंद्रस्तुराषाण्मित्रो न जघान वृत्रं यतिर्न । (*iṃdrasturāṣāṇmitro na jaghāna vṛtraṃ yatirna |*)" The purport of this mantra is that Indra killed Vṛtra now just as Mitra and Yati killed him before, and "बिभेद बलं भृगुर्न (*bibheda balaṃ bhṛgurna*)" that he killed Bala as Bhṛgu did. Besides, in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (7-28) it is said that Indra abandoned the yatis and made them offerings to wolves.
The purport of the 8th mantra gives the purport of the Atharvaveda sentence "अष्टयोनीरदितिरष्टपुत्रा (*aṣṭayonīraditiraṣṭaputrā*)" (A. Ve. 8-9-21). Stating the same idea in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka also, "अष्टयोनीमष्टपुत्रां । अष्टपत्नीमिमां महीं ॥ (*aṣṭayonīmaṣṭaputrāṃ | aṣṭapatnīmimāṃ mahīṃ ‖*)" (Tai. Ā. 1-13-1), it is stated that the earth (Aditi) has eight births, eight sons and eight husbands.
The 8th and 9th mantras of the 72nd sūkta are quoted also in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka. In place of the Saṃhitā reading "परा मार्तांडमास्यत् (*parā mārtāṃḍamāsyat*)" there is there the variant reading "परा मार्तांडमाभरत् (*parā mārtāṃḍamābharat*)". The commentator, explaining its meaning, has said as follows: "मार्तांडाख्यं अष्टमं पुत्रं ‘ परा भरत् ’ परि त्यक्तवती इति यत् तत् मृत्युवे मृत्युनिमित्तं (*mārtāṃḍākhyaṃ aṣṭamaṃ putraṃ ‘ parā bharat ’ pari tyaktavatī iti yat tat mṛtyuve mṛtyunimittaṃ*)". The purport is that as soon as Mārtāṇḍa was produced, the egg died.
Explaining the 8th mantra above, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa says as follows.
अष्टौ ह वै पुत्रा आदितेः । यांस्वेतद्देवा आदित्या इत्याचक्षते सप्त हैव तेऽविकृतग्ं हाष्टमं जनयांचकार मार्तांडग्ं संदेघो हैवास यावानेवोर्ध्वस्तावांस्तिर्यज् पुरुषसम्मित इत्यु हैक आहुः (*aṣṭau ha vai putrā āditeḥ | yāṃsvetaddevā ādityā ityācakṣate sapta haiva te 'vikṛtagṃ hāṣṭamaṃ janayāṃcakāra mārtāṃḍagṃ saṃdegho haivāsa yāvānevordhvastāvāṃstiryaj puruṣasammita ityu haika āhuḥ*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-1-3-3)
Aditi had eight sons. But of them only seven gods are called Ādityas by men. For the eighth, Mārtāṇḍa, without hands, feet and the like, without any special quality, endowed only with the quality of a lump (mārdana **NOTE**) and free of distinctions, grew as long as he was high and was born so; he became only of the measure of a man's body: such is the opinion of some.
त उ हैत ऊचुः । देवा आदित्या यदस्मानन्वजनिमा तदमुयेव भूद्धं. तेमं विकरवामेति तं विचक्रुर्यथायं पुरुषो विकृतस्तस्य यानि माग्‌ंसानि संस्कृत्य सन्यासुस्ततो हस्ती समभवत्तस्मादाहुर्न हस्तिनं प्रतिगृह्णीयात्पुरुषाचानो हि हस्तीति यमु ह तद्विचक्रुः स विवस्वानादित्यस्तस्येमाः प्रजाः ॥ (*ta u haita ūcuḥ | devā ādityā yadasmānanvajanimā tadamuyeva bhūddhaṃ. temaṃ vikaravāmeti taṃ vicakruryathāyaṃ puruṣo vikṛtastasya yāni māgṃsāni saṃskṛtya sanyāsustato hastī samabhavattasmādāhurna hastinaṃ pratigṛhṇīyātpuruṣācāno hi hastīti yamu ha tadvicakruḥ sa vivasvānādityastasyemāḥ prajāḥ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 3-1-3-4)

### Printed p. 38
Seeing Mārtāṇḍa thus without distinctions, the Ādityas were frightened. "If this Mārtāṇḍa does not resemble our form, then death is near to us. Therefore we must make him take a form," and so they transformed him into the shape of a man. When, in order to give him their form, they cut his body and set it right, and threw away the flesh taken out, an elephant was produced from it. This new person, endowed with special qualities such as name and form, they called Vivasvān; this Vivasvān became the cause of the origin of all beings.
The same sentence is quoted also in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, where it is said that this second embryo of Aditi took the form of an egg. (Tai. Saṃ. 6-5.6-1)
Yāska, explaining the purport of the mantra 10-22-4 **NOTE** above, has said in the sentences of the Nirukta as follows.
आदित्यो दक्ष इत्याहुः । आदित्यमध्ये च स्तुतः । अदितिः दाक्षायणी । अदितेर्दक्षो अजायत दक्षाद्वदितिः परि(१०-७२-४) इति। तत्कथमुपपद्येत । समानजन्मानौ स्यातां इति । अपि वा देवधर्मेणेतरेतरजन्मानौ स्यातामितरेतरप्रकृती ॥ (*ādityo dakṣa ityāhuḥ | ādityamadhye ca stutaḥ | aditiḥ dākṣāyaṇī | aditerdakṣo ajāyata dakṣādvaditiḥ pari(10-72-4) iti| tatkathamupapadyeta | samānajanmānau syātāṃ iti | api vā devadharmeṇetaretarajanmānau syātāmitaretaraprakṛtī ‖*) (Ni. 11-23)
Dakṣa is praised and renowned as one of the sons of Aditi and as belonging to the group of the Ādityas. But the sentence "From Dakṣa Aditi was born; from Aditi Dakṣa was born" makes known that Aditi is also the daughter of Dakṣa. How can this be possible? Both may have one and the same place of origin, or, in keeping with the natural power of the gods, one may have been born from the other, and they may have received greatness from one another. In the same way there is another Śruti sentence that states that each became the womb for the origin of the other.
"तस्माद्विराडजायत विराजो अधि पूरुषः (*tasmādvirāḍajāyata virājo adhi pūruṣaḥ*)" (10-90-5)
From the Puruṣa the Virāṭ was born; and that from the Virāṭ the Puruṣa was born is also well known; therefore the order of origin of Dakṣa and Aditi may be of the same kind. Other sentences setting out the mutual relation of Dakṣa and Aditi may also be examined. असच्च सच्च........ वृषभश्च धेनु: (*asacca sacca........ vṛṣabhaśca dhenu:*) (10-5-7)
1. "The principle that is unmanifest and possessed of the truth of the highest reality, being established in the seed of Dakṣa and in the womb of Aditi, then became a principle that includes the two powers in the form of female and male."

### Printed p. 39
2. "दक्षस्य वादिते जन्मनि........ विसुरूपेषु जन्मषु (*dakṣasya vādite janmani........ visurūpeṣu janmaṣu*)"
(10-64-5)
O Aditi, as soon as Dakṣa was born, and in accordance with his resolve, you nourished the two lords in the form of Mitra and Varuṇa.
Thus, although the mantras 10-72-4 and 5 state both relations together, that Dakṣa is father and son of Aditi, sentences such as 2-27-1 state clearly that Dakṣa is a son of Aditi and one of the Ādityas. Besides, in some other places the gods have the epithet "having Dakṣa as father". For example—
1. "सुज्योतिष: सूर्य दक्षपितृननागास्ते सुमहो वीहि देवान् (*sujyotiṣa: sūrya dakṣapitṛnanāgāste sumaho vīhi devān*)"
(6-50-2)
O great Sūrya, approach the gods who have Dakṣa as father. (Sāyaṇa here has explained the word father as grandfather. Since Aditi is the daughter of Dakṣa and the gods are sons of Aditi, Dakṣa is the grandfather of these gods.)
2. "या धारयंत देवाः सुदक्षा दक्षपितरा । असुराय प्रमहसा ॥ (*yā dhārayaṃta devāḥ sudakṣā dakṣapitarā | asurāya pramahasā ‖*)"
(7-66-2)
Mitra and Varuṇa, who have Dakṣa as father, have established their divine lordship, and so on.
In the same way, in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā also,
ये देवा मनोजाता मनोयुजः सुदक्षा दक्षपितारस्ते नः पांतु ॥ (*ye devā manojātā manoyujaḥ sudakṣā dakṣapitāraste naḥ pāṃtu ‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 1-2-3-1)
May the gods, mind-born, yoked by the mind, having Dakṣa himself as father, protect us.
In the examples above it cannot be said precisely that the word Dakṣa is the designation of a particular individual. In 8-25-5 Mitra and Varuṇa are described not only as "powerful sons of Dakṣa" (सूनू दक्षस्य सुक्रतू (*sūnū dakṣasya sukratū*)) but also as "grandsons of strength" (नपाता शवसो महः (*napātā śavaso mahaḥ*)). The way in which Sāyaṇa has explained the mantra 6.50-2 has been stated above. In the Ṛk 7-66-2 quoted above he has explained दक्षपितारा (*dakṣapitārā*) as बलस्य पालकौ (*balasya pālakau*), protectors of strength. The epithets सहसस्पुत्रः (*sahasasputraḥ*) and सहसःसूनुः (*sahasaḥsūnuḥ*) and the like are used in some places with reference to Agni (8-49-2; 8-60-11 and so on) and in some places with reference to Indra (8.79-3 and so on).

### Printed p. 40

In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa, in the passage 2.4.4-2, the identity of Dakṣa with Prajāpati, the creator, is shown. In 2-4-4.6 it is stated to be the name of a person who was a son of the mountain. **NOTE**
It will not be out of place at present to examine how Dakṣa is described in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, which has expanded the story relying on these Vedic passages. Three variant readings are seen in the edition of this Viṣṇu Purāṇa. They may be examined one by one.
(1) In the first version the account of the story is as follows:—Dakṣa is one of the mind-born sons of Brahmā. He marries a woman named Prasūti. By her he obtains twenty-four daughters. But it is not included that Aditi is one of them.
(2) In the second version:—Aditi is one of the sixty daughters of Dakṣa; he gave her in marriage to Kaśyapa together with Diti, Danu and ten others; and then the twelve Ādityas were born to Kaśyapa from Aditi.
(3) In the manner of the third narration:—it is known that Aditi is a daughter of Dakṣa and the mother of Vivasvat (the Sun). [That this same Aditi is the wife of Kaśyapa and the mother of Viṣṇu who took the Vāmana incarnation is known from a context in the Rāmāyaṇa (here too there are variant readings).]
In the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā (Vā. Saṃ. 29-60) and in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 7-5-14-1) Aditi is stated to be the wife of Viṣṇu. (आदित्यै विष्णुपत्नै चरुः (*ādityai viṣṇupatnai caruḥ*)) In the following mantra quoted from the Taittirīya Saṃhitā also the description of the nature of Aditi is as follows.
विष्टंभो दिवो धरुणः पृथिव्या अस्येशाना जगतो विष्णु पत्नि । विश्वव्यचा इषयंती सुभूतिः शिवा नो अस्त्वदितिरुपस्थे ॥ (*viṣṭaṃbho divo dharuṇaḥ pṛthivyā asyeśānā jagato viṣṇu patni | viśvavyacā iṣayaṃtī subhūtiḥ śivā no astvaditirupasthe ‖*)
Aditi, who is as a support to the world of heaven, the upholder of the earth, the mistress of the whole world, the wife of Viṣṇu, all-pervading, endowed with power, may she bring welfare to us who rest in her lap.
In all these contexts it is clear that Aditi is the chief cause of the creation of the world and of its sustenance.

### Printed p. 41

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अदि॑ति॒र्ह्यज॑निष्ट॒ दक्ष॒ या दु॑हि॒ता तव॑ ।  
> तां दे॒वा अन्व॑जायंत भ॒द्रा अ॒मृत॑बंधवः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अदितिः । हि । अजनिष्ट । दक्ष । या । दुहिता । तव ।  
> तां । देवाः । अनु । अजायंत । भद्राः । अमृतबंथवः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अदितिदेवताके पशावदितिर्ह्यजनिष्टेत्येषा हविषोऽनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च ।
> अदितिर्ह्यजनिष्प सुत्रामाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं ॥ आ. ३-८ ॥ इति ॥
> हे दक्ष तव या दुहिताभूत् सादितिरजनिष्ट हि पुत्रानादित्यान् । तदेवाह ।
> तां देवा अन्वजायंत भद्राः स्तुत्या भजनीया अमृतबंधवोऽमरणबंधनाः ॥१॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the animal sacrifice (paśu) addressed to Aditi, this verse is the anuvākyā (invitatory) of the oblation; the Sūtra says so: अदितिर्ह्यजनिष्प सुत्रामाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं (*aditirhyajaniṣpa sutrāmāṇaṃ pṛthivīṃ dyāmanehasaṃ*) (Ā. 3-8). Sāyaṇa explains: O Dakṣa, she who was your daughter, that Aditi bore sons, the Ādityas. He says the same again: after her the gods were born, भद्राः (*bhadrāḥ*), that is, praiseworthy and worthy to be served, अमृतबंधवः (*amṛtabaṃdhavaḥ*), that is, those whose bonds are not those of death.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दक्ष (*dakṣa*)—O Dakṣa, divine one
- या (*yā*)—who
- तव (*tava*)—to you
- दुहिता (*duhitā*)—daughter, such
- अदिति: (*aditi:*)—Aditi
- अजनिष्ट हि (*ajaniṣṭa hi*)—bore sons, the Ādityas and others
- तां अनु (*tāṃ anu*)—following her
- भद्राः (*bhadrāḥ*)—auspicious
- अमृतबंधवः (*amṛtabaṃdhavaḥ*)—free from the bond in the form of death
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—other gods
- अजायंत (*ajāyaṃta*)—were born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Dakṣa, your daughter Aditi bore sons, the Ādityas and others. After her were born other gods, auspicious and free from the bond in the form of death.

##### English translation
Aditi, who was your daughter, Daksha, was born; after her the gods were born adorable, freed from the bonds of death.

### Printed p. 42

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्दे॑वा अ॒दः स॑लि॒ले सुसं॑रब्धा॒ अति॑ष्ठत ।  
> अत्रा॑ वो॒ नृत्य॑तामित ती॒व्रो रे॒णुरपा॑यत ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । देवाः । अदः । सलिले । सुसंरब्धाः । अतिष्ठत ।  
> अत्र । वः । नृत्यतां इव । तीव्रः । रेणुः : अप । आयत ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनयोत्तरेण चादित्याः स्तूयंते । यद्यदा हे देवाः अदोऽमुष्मिन्सलिले यूयं सुसंरब्धाः सुष्टु लब्धात्मानोऽतिष्ठत स्थितवंतः ! आपो वा इदं
> सर्वं । तै. आ. १०.२२ । अप एव ससर्जादाविति श्रुतिस्मृती । अत्रास्मिन्सलिले नृत्यतामिव
> वो युष्माकं संबंधी तीव्रो दुःसहो रेणुरंशभूत एकोपायत । अपागच्छत् । दिवं प्रति गत इति सूर्याभिप्रायं । परा मार्तांडमास्यदिति वक्ष्यति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

By this verse and the one that follows, the Ādityas are praised. यत् (*yat*) means "when": O gods, when you stood in that water, सुसंरब्धाः (*susaṃrabdhāḥ*), that is, well established, having well obtained your own being. The Śruti and Smṛti say: आपो वा इदं सर्वं (*āpo vā idaṃ sarvaṃ*) (Tai. Ā. 10.22) and अप एव ससर्जादाविति (*apa eva sasarjādāviti*), that is, in the beginning he created the waters only. Here, in this water, a fierce, hard to endure dust, belonging to you as if you were dancing, being a part (of you), went forth; it went away, went towards the sky; this has the Sun in view. He will say further that the Sun was cast away.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अदः सलिले (*adaḥ salile*)—in this pool
- सुसंरब्धाः (*susaṃrabdhāḥ*)—having an excellent mind
- अतिष्ठत (*atiṣṭhata*)—you stood, then
- अत्र (*atra*)—in this same pool
- नृत्यतांइव (*nṛtyatāṃiva*)—as if you were dancing
- वः (*vaḥ*)—belonging to you
- तीव्रः (*tīvraḥ*)—and sharp
- रेणुः (*reṇuḥ*)—a part of radiance
- अपायत (*apāyata*)—arose

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, when you stood in this pool having an excellent mind, then in this same pool, as if you were dancing, a part of radiance belonging to you, and sharp, arose.

##### English translation
When, gods, you abode in this pool well-arranged, then a pungent dust went forth from you as if you were dancing.

### Printed p. 43

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्दे॑वा॒ यत॑यो यथा॒ भुव॑ना॒न्यपि॑न्वत ।  
> अत्रा॑ समु॒द्र आ गू॒ळ्हमा सूर्य॑मजभर्तन ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । देवाः । यतयः । यथा । भुवनानि । अपिन्वत ।  
> अत्र । समुद्रे । आ । गूळ्हं । आ । सूर्यं । अजभर्तन ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा हे देवाः यतयो यथा । वृष्ट्वा नियमयंतीति वा वर्षणेन
> यातयंतीति वा यतयो मेघाः । ते यथोदकैर्भुवनानि लोकं पूरयंति
> तद्वत्स्वतेजोभिरपिन्वत । पूरितवंतः । अत्र समुद्रेऽप्स्वा गूळं निगूळं सूर्यं
> प्रातरुदयायाजभर्तन । आहृतवंतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

यत् (*yat*) means "when": O gods, like यतयः (*yatayaḥ*). The यतयः (*yatayaḥ*) are the clouds, either because after raining they restrain (the world), or because they cause it to strive by raining. As they fill the worlds, the earth, with waters, so you filled the worlds with your own radiance. Here, in the ocean, that is, in the waters, you brought the Sun, hidden and concealed, for the sake of his rising in the morning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)—the worlds
- यतयोयथा (*yatayoyathā*)—as clouds fill with rain
- अपिन्वत (*apinvata*)—when you filled these worlds with your radiance, then
- अत्रसमुद्रे (*atrasamudre*)—in this mass of water
- आ गूळं (*ā gūḷaṃ*)—hidden, concealed
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—the Sun
- अ अजभर्तन (*a ajabhartana*)—you brought out and made him shine

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, when you filled these worlds with your radiance as clouds fill the worlds with rain, then you brought out and made shine the Sun hidden in this mass of water.

##### English translation
When, gods, you filled the worlds (with your radiance) as clouds (fill the earth with rain), then you brought forth the sun hidden in the ocean.

### Printed p. 44

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒ष्टौ पु॒त्रासो॒ अदि॑ते॒र्ये जा॒तास्त॒न्व१॒ं॑स्परि॑ ।  
> दे॒वा उप॒ प्रैत्स॒प्तभिः॒ परा॑ मार्तां॒डमा॑स्यत् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अष्टौ । पुत्रासः । आदितेः । ये । जाताः । तन्वः । परि ।  
> देवान् । उप । प्र । ऐत् । सप्तभिः । परा । मार्तांडं । अस्यत् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अष्टौ पुत्रासः पुत्रा मित्रादयोऽदितेर्भवंति येऽदितेस्तन्वः परि शरीराज्ञाता उत्पन्नाः । अदितेरष्टौ पुत्रा अध्वर्युब्राह्मणे परिगणिताः । तथा हि । ताननुक्रमिष्यामो मित्रश्च वरुणश्च धाता चार्यमा चांशश्च भगश्च विवस्वानादित्यश्चेति ।
> तथा तत्त्रैव प्रदेशांतरेऽदितिं प्रस्तुत्याम्नातं । तस्या उच्चेषणमददुस्तत्पाश्नात्
> सा रेतोऽधत्त तस्यॆ चत्वार आदित्या अजायंत सा द्वितीयमपचदित्यष्टानामादित्यानामुत्पत्तिर्वणीता । तै. सं. ६-५-६-१ । सादितिः सप्तभिः पुत्रैर्देवानुप
> प्रैत् । उपागच्छत् । अष्टमं पुत्रं मार्तांडं सूर्यं परास्यत् । उपरि प्राक्षिपदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अष्टौ पुत्रासः (*aṣṭau putrāsaḥ*): the eight sons, Mitra and the others, who were born तन्वः परि (*tanvaḥ pari*), from the body of Aditi. The eight sons of Aditi are counted in the Adhvaryu Brāhmaṇa; it is so: "We shall enumerate them: Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātā, Aryamā, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Vivasvān and Āditya." Likewise in another place in the same (work), speaking of Aditi, it is recited: तस्या उच्चेषणमददुस्तत्पाश्नात् सा रेतोऽधत्त तस्यॆ चत्वार आदित्या अजायंत (*tasyā ucceṣaṇamadadustatpāśnāt sā reto 'dhatta tasyè catvāra ādityā ajāyaṃta*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-5-6-1) **GLOSS** Thus the origin of the eight Ādityas is described. That Aditi approached the gods with seven sons; the eighth son, Mārtāṇḍa, the Sun, she cast away, that is, threw upward.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ये (*ye*)—those gods, Mitra and the others
- अदितेः (*aditeḥ*)—of Aditi
- तन्वः परि (*tanvaḥ pari*)—from the body
- जाताः (*jātāḥ*)—who were born, such
- पुत्रासः (*putrāsaḥ*)—the sons of Aditi
- अष्टौ (*aṣṭau*)—eight in number
- सप्तभिः पुत्रैः (*saptabhiḥ putraiḥ*)—with seven sons among them
- देवान् (*devān*)—to the gods
- उप प्रैत् (*upa prait*)—went and approached
- मार्तांडं (*mārtāṃḍaṃ*)—the Sun
- परास्यत् (*parāsyat*)—threw to the upper region (established in the atmosphere)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The gods, Mitra and the others, who were born from the body of Aditi, are such sons of Aditi, eight in number. With seven sons among them she approached the gods; the Sun she sent upward and established in the atmosphere. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 45

##### English translation
Eight sons (there were) of Aditi who were born from her body; she approached the gods with seven, she sent forth Martanda on high.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk and in the next it is said that Aditi has eight sons. In some places seven are mentioned. Although there is a custom of calling them collectively Ādityas, all of them have separate names. Of these, the matter of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā will be explained first, and afterwards such matters as how many Ādityas there are and how they were born will be examined.
Although these three deities, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, are conceived as separate deities, it seems that they are described here as many forms of the Sun, that is, as part-deities bearing these names among the twelve Ādityas. Besides, in some places it is said that these three deities are other forms of Agni:
त्वमग्ने वरुणो जायसे यत्त्वं मित्रो भवसि यत्समिद्धः । (*tvamagne varuṇo jāyase yattvaṃ mitro bhavasi yatsamiddhaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3-1)
त्वमर्यमा भवसि यत्कनीनां नाम स्वधावन् गुह्यं बिभर्षि ॥ (*tvamaryamā bhavasi yatkanīnāṃ nāma svadhāvan guhyaṃ bibharṣi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3-2)
In these Ṛks it is said that when Agni is born he is called Varuṇa, when he is well kindled and blazing he is called Mitra, and in the homas and the like connected with a maiden he is called Aryamā; Agni has these three names. Besides, in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa:—
मित्रोऽसि वरुणोऽसीत्याह । मैत्रं ना अहः। वारुणी रात्रिः। अहोरात्राभ्यामेवैनमुपावहरति ॥ (*mitro 'si varuṇo 'sītyāha | maitraṃ nā ahaḥ| vāruṇī rātriḥ| ahorātrābhyāmevainamupāvaharati ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-7-10-1)
it is said that Mitra is the lord of the day and Varuṇa the lord of the night. Therefore it should be noted that these three deities are said in some places to be part-deities of the Sun and in some places different names of Agni.
In the Purāṇas and elsewhere Mitra is one of the twelve Ādityas. Although in the Ṛgveda Mitra is another name of the Sun, Mitra has a distinct individuality, that is, a personal importance. In the Ṛgveda Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā are said to be sons of Aditi. Therefore in many places they are called Ādityas. These Ādityas, that is, the sons of Aditi, being six in number—

### Printed p. 46

इमा गिर आदित्येभ्यो धृतस्नूः सनाद्राजभ्यो जुह्वा जुहोमि । शृणोतु मित्रो अर्यमा भगो नस्तु विजातो वरुणो दक्षो अंशः ॥ (*imā gira ādityebhyo dhṛtasnūḥ sanādrājabhyo juhvā juhomi | śṛṇotu mitro aryamā bhago nastu vijāto varuṇo dakṣo aṃśaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1)
that is, only the names of six persons are given: Mitra, Aryamā, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa, Aṃśa.
देवा आदित्या ये सप्त तेभिः सोमाभि रक्ष न इंद्रायेंदो परि स्रव । (*devā ādityā ye sapta tebhiḥ somābhi rakṣa na iṃdrāyeṃdo pari srava |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-114-3)
In this verse the sons of Aditi are said to be seven persons—
अष्टौ पुत्रासो आदितेर्ये जातास्तन्व १ स्परि । देवा उपप्रैत्सप्तभिः परा मार्तांडमास्यत् । सप्तभिः पुत्रैरदितिरुप प्रैत्तूव्यं युगं । प्रजायै मृत्यवे त्वत्पुनमार्तांडमाभरत् ॥ (*aṣṭau putrāso āditerye jātāstanva 1 spari | devā upapraitsaptabhiḥ parā mārtāṃḍamāsyat | saptabhiḥ putrairaditirupa praittūvyaṃ yugaṃ | prajāyai mṛtyave tvatpunamārtāṃḍamābharat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-72.8, 9)
In these Ṛks it is said that Aditi had first seven sons and afterwards Mārtāṇḍa, eight sons in all. Besides, in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka—
अष्टौ पुत्रासो अदितेः । ये जातास्तन्वः परि । देवा उपप्रैत्सप्तभिः । परामार्तांडमास्यत् । सप्तभिः पुत्रैरदितिः । उपप्रैत्तूर्व्यं युगं । प्रजायैमृत्यवे तत् । परामार्तांडमाभरदिति । ताननुक्रमिष्यामः । मित्रश्च वरुणश्च । धाता चार्यमा च । अगंशश्च भगश्च । इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्श्चेत्येते॥ (*aṣṭau putrāso aditeḥ | ye jātāstanvaḥ pari | devā upapraitsaptabhiḥ | parāmārtāṃḍamāsyat | saptabhiḥ putrairaditiḥ | upapraittūrvyaṃ yugaṃ | prajāyaimṛtyave tat | parāmārtāṃḍamābharaditi | tānanukramiṣyāmaḥ | mitraśca varuṇaśca | dhātā cāryamā ca | agaṃśaśca bhagaśca | iṃdraśca vivasvāgścetyete‖*) (Tai. Ā. 1-13-3)
the names of the eight sons that Aditi had are also given. It is said that Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātā, Aryamā, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Indra and Vivasvān are those eight persons. The same matter in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa—
अदितिः पुत्रकामा । साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यै धाता चार्यमा चाजायेतां । सा द्वितीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यै मित्रश्च वरुणश्चाजायेतां । सा तृतीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्या अगंंशश्च भगश्चाजायेतां । सा चतुर्थमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्या इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्ंश्चाजायेतां ॥ (*aditiḥ putrakāmā | sādhyebhyo devebhyo brahmaudanamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyai dhātā cāryamā cājāyetāṃ | sā dvitīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyai mitraśca varuṇaścājāyetāṃ | sā tṛtīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyā agaṃṃśaśca bhagaścājāyetāṃ | sā caturthamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyā iṃdraśca vivasvāgṃścājāyetāṃ ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-1-9.1, 2. 3)

### Printed p. 47

is stated. That Aditi obtained eight sons is clearly described here. Of these eight, only the three, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, are especially famous in the Ṛgveda. In the 2-7th sūkta of the Ṛ. Saṃ. there is an extensive description of the deeds of valour of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, beneficial to the world. **NOTE**
Mitra:—The derivation of the word Mitra has been given by Yāska in his Nirukta as follows—
मित्रः प्रमीतेस्त्रायते । संमिन्वानो द्रवतीति वा । मेदयतीर्वा । (*mitraḥ pramītestrāyate | saṃminvāno dravatīti vā | medayatīrvā |*) (Ni. 10-21)
If it is asked what the meaning of the word Mitra is—
मित्रः कस्मात् । स हि प्रमितेः त्रायते । प्रमितिः मरणं । ततः सर्वलोकं त्रायते वर्षद्वारेण । (*mitraḥ kasmāt | sa hi pramiteḥ trāyate | pramitiḥ maraṇaṃ | tataḥ sarvalokaṃ trāyate varṣadvāreṇa |*)
Pramiti means death. Because he brings about creation for the people in the world, and thereby provides food and other nourishment, and protects them by averting the fear of death that may arise from hunger, this deity is called Mitra.
समंततो मिन्वानः उदकेन द्रवति अंतरिक्षलोके (*samaṃtato minvānaḥ udakena dravati aṃtarikṣaloke*) This deity, being the lord of water and staying in the atmosphere, makes, by means of the clouds, all around, that is, all regions, become drenched, that is, wet with water; therefore this deity is called Mitra.
Or:—स्नेहनार्थस्य सर्वं ह्यसावुदकेन स्नेहयति (*snehanārthasya sarvaṃ hyasāvudakena snehayati*) This deity makes everything cling by water, that is, unite and join together; therefore this deity is called Mitra. Having stated these three kinds of derivation, in order to support it he has given as an example the Ṛk—
मित्रो जनान् यातयति ब्रुवाणो मित्रो दाधार पृथवीमुत द्यां । मित्रः कृष्टीरनिमिषाभि चष्टे मित्राय हव्यं धृतवज्जु होत ॥ (*mitro janān yātayati bruvāṇo mitro dādhāra pṛthavīmuta dyāṃ | mitraḥ kṛṣṭīranimiṣābhi caṣṭe mitrāya havyaṃ dhṛtavajju hota ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59-1)
In this Ṛk the extraordinary qualities of Mitra and his benefit to the world are described very well: helping in agriculture and the like, pouring down rain and the like and providing the people with food and other nourishment, he sustains, that is, nourishes, the people of the earth and the dwellers in heaven. O men, offer oblations and the like mixed with ghee to such a divine Mitra. The only sūkta in the Ṛgveda in praise of Mitra is this sūkta of 9 Ṛks (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59); elsewhere Mitra is praised together with other deities such as Varuṇa. Because the association of Mitra and Varuṇa is very frequent, as said before there is only one sūkta in the Ṛgveda in which Mitra alone is the deity (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59). There too, of that deity the

### Printed p. 48

praise portion is obscure. But only in the first Ṛk is the distinctive character of Mitra well stated. In the fifth Ṛk of the same sūkta—
महा आदित्यो नमसोपसद्यो यातयज्जनो गृणते सुशेवः । (*mahā ādityo namasopasadyo yātayajjano gṛṇate suśevaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59-5)
this Mitra is called a venerable Āditya who gathers all men together. The word यातयज्जन (*yātayajjana*), which gives this meaning, is used with reference to Mitra and Varuṇa in the Ṛks—
व्रतेन स्थो ध्रुवक्षेमा धर्मणा यातयज्जना (*vratena stho dhruvakṣemā dharmaṇā yātayajjanā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-77-2) **GLOSS**
and with reference to the three, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, in the Ṛk—
मित्रस्तयोर्वरुणो यातयज्जनोऽर्यमा यातयज्जनः (*mitrastayorvaruṇo yātayajjano 'ryamā yātayajjanaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-136-3) **GLOSS**
According to the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59 mentioned above, Mitra is the support of heaven and earth. The five groups of men (पंचजनाः (*paṃcajanāḥ*)) are obedient to him, and he is the support of life for all the other deities; thus the qualities of Mitra are described. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-81-4 it is said that Savitṛ and Mitra are one; in the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā (Vā. Saṃ. 4-3) that Viṣṇu took three steps following the rules of Mitra; in Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-4 that Agni, established before the time of dawn, himself produces Mitra for himself; in Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-5-4 that the kindled Agni is Mitra; in Ṛ. Saṃ. 4 3-1 that when Agni is born he is Mitra, **NOTE**
मित्रो अग्निर्भवति यत्समिद्दो मित्रो होता वरुणो जातवेदाः । मित्रो अध्वर्युरिषिरो दमूना मित्रः सिंधूनामुत पर्वतानां ॥ (*mitro agnirbhavati yatsamiddo mitro hotā varuṇo jātavedāḥ | mitro adhvaryuriṣiro damūnā mitraḥ siṃdhūnāmuta parvatānāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-5-4)
In this verse there is a description of the qualities of Mitra: that the well-kindled Agni is Mitra, that this Mitra is in the sacrifice the Hotṛ, the Adhvaryu, Varuṇa and the Agni called Jātavedas, and that he is the friend (benefactor) of the rivers and the mountains.
Varuṇa:—वरुणो वृणोतीति सतः (*varuṇo vṛṇotīti sataḥ*) (Ni. 10-2) Yāska's derivation is that because he is covered by a mass of clouds this deity is called Varuṇa.

### Printed p. 49

Varuṇa is a most exalted deity, equal to Indra in power and the like. The number of mantras addressed to Varuṇa is very small; there are twelve sūktas in the Ṛgveda addressed to Varuṇa. But the sūktas that praise Mitra and Varuṇa together are twenty-four.
Varuṇa has a human body and human activities. But rather than his physical body and activities, the description of his moral activities and deeds is what is chiefly suggested. He has a face, eyes, arms, hands and feet; he drives a chariot, sits, eats, drinks and so on; there is a description of his bodily activities. That the Sun is the eye of Agni, Mitra and Varuṇa—
चित्रं देवानामुदगादनीकं चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्याग्नेः (*citraṃ devānāmudagādanīkaṃ cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasyāgneḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-115-1)
उदु त्यच्चक्षमहि मित्रयोरा एति प्रियं वरुणयोरदब्धं । (*udu tyaccakṣamahi mitrayorā eti priyaṃ varuṇayoradabdhaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Sa. 6-51-6) **NOTE**
उद्वां चक्षुर्वरुण सुप्रतीकं देवयोरेति सूर्यस्ततन्वान् । (*udvāṃ cakṣurvaruṇa supratīkaṃ devayoreti sūryastatanvān |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-61 1) **NOTE**
चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्य देवश्चर्मेव यः समविव्यक् तमांसि । (*cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasya devaścarmeva yaḥ samavivyak tamāṃsi |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-63-1)
नमो मित्रस्य वरुणस्य चक्षसे महो देवाय तदृतं सपर्यत । (*namo mitrasya varuṇasya cakṣase maho devāya tadṛtaṃ saparyata |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-37-1)
is stated clearly in these Ṛks. Since the statement that the Sun is as an eye to him stands in the very first Ṛk of the sūktas addressed to Mitra and Varuṇa, it appears that this is the point that first comes to mind whenever Mitra and Varuṇa are mentioned. And in the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 7.66-10 it is said that Aryamā, Mitra and Varuṇa have the Sun as their eye. That Varuṇa has far-reaching sight (उरुचक्षसं (*urucakṣasaṃ*)) is said in Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-5; 1-25-16.
आ नो बर्हि रिशादसो वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा । सीदंतु मनुषो यथा ॥ (*ā no barhi riśādaso varuṇo mitro aryamā | sīdaṃtu manuṣo yathā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-26-4)
व्रतेन स्थो ध्रुवक्षेमा धर्मणा यातॆयॆज्जना । नि बर्हिषि सदतं सोमपीतये ॥ (*vratena stho dhruvakṣemā dharmaṇā yātèyèjjanā | ni barhiṣi sadataṃ somapītaye ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. - 5-72-2) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 50

In Ṛks such as these, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā are prayed to come and sit on the darbha grass spread on the sacrificial altar for the drinking of Soma.
यदी सखाया सख्याय सोमैः सुतेभिः सुप्रयसा मादय्यैते । (*yadī sakhāyā sakhyāya somaiḥ sutebhiḥ suprayasā mādayyaite |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4.41-3)
In Ṛks such as these it is said that Varuṇa drinks Soma along with Indra and the other deities.
भिभ्रद्दापिं हिरण्ययं वरुणो वस्त्र निर्णिजं । परि स्पशो नि षेदिरे । (*bhibhraddāpiṃ hiraṇyayaṃ varuṇo vastra nirṇijaṃ | pari spaśo ni ṣedire |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-13)
In this Ṛk there is a description that Varuṇa wears a golden mantle or robe and a shining garment. The chariot of Varuṇa is golden, shining like the Sun, and it has whips that urge on the horses; Mitra and Varuṇa mount the chariot in a very high region of the sky (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5 63-1) and there are such descriptions. **NOTE** The dwelling of Mitra and Varuṇa is golden and it is in the upper world. And Varuṇa, seated in his palace—
नि षसाद धृतव्रतो वरुणः पस्ता ३ स्वा । साम्राज्याय सुकृतुः । अतो विश्वान्यद्भुता चिकित्त्वा अभि पश्यति । कृतानि या च कर्त्वा ॥ (*ni ṣasāda dhṛtavrato varuṇaḥ pastā 3 svā | sāmrājyāya sukṛtuḥ | ato viśvānyadbhutā cikittvā abhi paśyati | kṛtāni yā ca kartvā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-1) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-10, 11) **NOTE**
sees everything that happens in the world. The dwelling-place of Mitra and Varuṇa is very large, very high, firm, resting on a thousand pillars. And their house has a thousand doors (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-88-5). The all-seeing Sun, setting out from his own abode, goes to the house of Mitra and Varuṇa to report the doings of men, and enters their dear house. There are also such descriptions.
Varuṇa, alone or together with Mitra, is called king in many places, like the other deities.
अबुध्ने राजा वरुणो वनस्योर्ध्वं स्तूपं ददते पूतदक्षः । (*abudhne rājā varuṇo vanasyordhvaṃ stūpaṃ dadate pūtadakṣaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-24-7)
त्वं विश्वेषां वरुणासि राजा ये च देवा असुर ये च मर्ताः । (*tvaṃ viśveṣāṃ varuṇāsi rājā ye ca devā asura ye ca martāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-10)
तेन विश्वस्य भुवनस्य राजा यवं न वृष्टिर्व्युनत्ति भूम । (*tena viśvasya bhuvanasya rājā yavaṃ na vṛṣṭirvyunatti bhūma |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-3)

### Printed p. 51

In Ṛks such as these it is said that Varuṇa is king not only of men but also of the gods and of all the worlds. Besides Varuṇa, the deities Indra, Yama, Soma and others also have the honorific name of king.
There are many stories connected with Indra. But in the case of Varuṇa it is quite the opposite: there is not a single story. Varuṇa is highly praised as the regulator of the physical and moral orders (वरुणस्य व्रतानि (*varuṇasya vratāni*)). The divine power of Mitra and Varuṇa is described by the word माया (*māyā*). When this is applied to the gods it means the hidden power they have to do good, and when it is applied to the demons the same word means the crooked power they have to do evil. In the good sense this word is fitting for Mitra and Varuṇa; in the bad sense it applies to demons. Like the meaning of the word असुर (*asura*), the word माया (*māyā*) too is used in both senses. By this māyā power hidden in himself, Varuṇa, standing in the atmosphere, measures the earth with the Sun as with a measuring rod. He makes the Sun cross the atmosphere and makes him dim with rain clouds. Then drops mixed with honey (sweet) fall (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-63-4). He makes the sky rain. Therefore this word माया (*māyā*) is said chiefly of Varuṇa among the deities in all the Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-48-14; 7-28-4; 10-99. 10; 10-147-5.
It is Varuṇa who lays down the laws of nature. It is he who established earth and heaven. And in the Ṛk—
आसीदद्विश्वा भुवनानि संराड्विश्वेत्तानि वरुणस्य व्रतानि (*āsīdadviśvā bhuvanāni saṃrāḍviśvettāni varuṇasya vratāni*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-42-1)
it is said that Varuṇa dwells in all the worlds and that his ordinances and the like are observed everywhere there; and in
तिस्रो द्यावो निहिता अंतरस्मिन् तिस्रो भूमिरुपराः षड्विधानाः । (*tisro dyāvo nihitā aṃtarasmin tisro bhūmiruparāḥ ṣaḍvidhānāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-47-5)
it is said that the three upper worlds and the three earthly worlds (lower worlds) are in his power. Varuṇa and Mitra rule the whole universe (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-63-7). They are the protectors of the whole world (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-4). Varuṇa is the support of heaven, earth and the sky (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-69-1, 4). He has placed Agni in the water, the Sun in the sky, and Soma on the stone. He has made a broad path for the Sun. Varuṇa, Mitra and Arya

### Printed p. 52

mā clear the way for the Sun (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-60-4). In many Ṛks such as these there is a description of the authority of Varuṇa.
In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 6-4-8-4) मित्रोऽहरजनयद्वरुणो रात्रिं (*mitro 'harajanayadvaruṇo rātriṃ*) that is, it is said that Mitra created the day and Varuṇa the night. Therefore मैत्रं वा अहः वारुणी रात्रिः (*maitraṃ vā ahaḥ vāruṇī rātriḥ*) that is, it is said in many places that Mitra is the lord of the day and Varuṇa the lord of the night (Tai. Saṃ. 2-1-7-4). In one place it is said that Varuṇa divided the seasons.
वेद मासो धृतव्रतो द्वादश प्रजावतः । वेदा य उपजायते । (*veda māso dhṛtavrato dvādaśa prajāvataḥ | vedā ya upajāyate |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-8)
In this it is said that Varuṇa knows the twelve months; and
वि ये दधुः शरदं मासनादहर्यज्ञमक्तुं चादृचं । अनाप्यं वरुणो मित्रो आर्यमा क्षत्रं राजान आशत ॥ (*vi ye dadhuḥ śaradaṃ māsanādaharyajñamaktuṃ cādṛcaṃ | anāpyaṃ varuṇo mitro āryamā kṣatraṃ rājāna āśata ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-66-11)
in this Ṛk it is said that Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā divided the autumn, the months, the day and the night.
That it is he who also regulates the waters is said in many places; it is he who made the rivers flow. By his command they flow continuously (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-28-4). That the rivers, though they hurriedly bring water and pour it into the ocean, do not fill it, is by his hidden power (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-6). Varuṇa and Mitra are the lords of the rivers. That Varuṇa is the lord of the oceans is an established point, but it occurs very rarely in the Ṛgveda. Perhaps the reason is that it is unimportant. In the Purāṇas and the like Varuṇa is famous as the lord of waters. And what is said in the Purāṇas and the like, that Varuṇa, as one of the eight guardians of the quarters, is the lord of the western quarter, has no support at all in the Ṛgveda. Generally, whatever is connected with Varuṇa is connected with the water that is with the atmosphere. Among the deities prayed to for rain, Varuṇa and Mitra are the foremost. When all the mountains are covered with clouds, Varuṇa, from an overturned vessel (a cloud), pours water on heaven, earth and sky and makes the whole earth wet; this is said in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-3, 4. One whole sūkta (that is, the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-63) indicates their rain-pouring power. In the Yajurveda Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 5-5-4-1) it is said आपो वरुणस्य पत्नय आसन् (*āpo varuṇasya patnaya āsan*)—the waters were the wives of Varuṇa—and (Tai. Saṃ. 6-4-3-2) मित्रावरुणौ वा अपां नेतार् (*mitrāvaruṇau vā apāṃ netār*)—Mitra and Varuṇa are the leaders of the waters.

### Printed p. 53

It is said again and again that the edicts of Varuṇa are well established. For this very reason he is called Dhṛtavrata. This epithet is fitting for both Mitra and Varuṇa.
परि धामानि मर्मृशद्वरुजस्य पुरो गये विश्वे देवा अनु व्रतं नभंतामन्यके समे ॥ (*pari dhāmāni marmṛśadvarujasya puro gaye viśve devā anu vrataṃ nabhaṃtāmanyake same ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-41-7)
न वां देवा अमृता आ मिनंति व्रतानि मित्रावरुणा ध्रुवाणि । (*na vāṃ devā amṛtā ā minaṃti vratāni mitrāvaruṇā dhruvāṇi |*) (Mi. Sa. 5-69-4) **NOTE**
In Ṛks such as these it is said that the gods themselves obey the commands of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, and that even the primeval gods cannot transgress their steadfast ordinances. Mitra and Varuṇa are the regulators of law (ṛta) and of light. It is by the strength of these edicts that they are able to give support to the laws. This quality is found especially in these two, and now and then also in the Ādityas or in other ordinary deities. The epithet ऋतस्य गोपाः (*ṛtasya gopāḥ*), that is, those who keep the laws (or make them be kept), as promoters of truth (ṛta) and justice, is often found applied to Mitra and Varuṇa.
The power of Varuṇa is unfathomable. Neither flying birds nor flowing rivers can reach the limit of the border of his realm; they cannot overcome his power; they cannot restrain his anger. **NOTE** The boundary limit of the realm of Mitra and Varuṇa has not been reached by the sky or by the rivers. Everything is hidden in Varuṇa. The dwellings of all beings, the three heavenly worlds and the three earthly worlds are placed in him alone. Varuṇa is all-knowing. The flight of birds in the sky, the course of ships in the ocean, the movement of the far-travelling wind, all this is known to him. He sees all things that are past or yet to come. He is the witness of the true and false deeds of men. All the deeds and devices of every man are known to Varuṇa. He can grasp all that is in heaven and the earthly worlds and beyond them. However far one may run beyond the sky, no one can leave the range of Varuṇa's sight.
As he is the regulator of moral law, sin makes Varuṇa very angry. He also becomes angry at the transgression of his laws; for both he imposes very cruel punishment. The noose of Varuṇa that binds sinners is spoken of in Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-24-15; 1-25-11; 6-74-4; 10-85-24. That noose binds the speaker of untruth; it lets go the speaker of truth.

### Printed p. 54

युवो राष्ट्रं बृहदिन्वति द्यौर्यौ सेतृभिररज्जुभिः सिनीथः । परि नो हेळो वरुणस्य वृज्या उरुं न इंद्रः कृणवदु लोकं ॥ (*yuvo rāṣṭraṃ bṛhadinvati dyauryau setṛbhirarajjubhiḥ sinīthaḥ | pari no heḷo varuṇasya vṛjyā uruṃ na iṃdraḥ kṛṇavadu lokaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-84-3)
In this Ṛk it is said that Varuṇa, accompanied by Indra, binds with bonds that are not ropes. The word pāśa (noose) occurs in the whole Ṛgveda in only one place, in a mantra addressed to Agni; and that too in a context in which it is prayed that the noose that binds his devotees be loosened. Therefore it may be said that these acts of binding with the noose and of release (loosening the noose) belong to Varuṇa alone. Varuṇa, together with Mitra, is the one who drives away, hates and punishes untruth. Mitra and Varuṇa punish those who are careless about their worship. But Varuṇa forgives those who repent; he unties like a rope; and he removes sin. Not only from sin committed by themselves, he releases men also from sin committed by their forefathers. He saves even those who daily transgress the laws laid down by him and afterwards repent. And he shows compassion to those who have transgressed his commands through forgetfulness. Varuṇa has hundreds and thousands of medicines. He drives away death and removes sin. He can take away life, and he can also give life. He is very skilful in protecting eternity. Thus the description of the qualities of Varuṇa is in many Ṛks. Here we have explained it very briefly. On a suitable occasion we shall explain it at length.
In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā—फल्गुनीनक्षत्रमर्यमा देवता..... .... .... अनुराधा नक्षत्रं मित्रो देवता .... ..... .... ..... (*phalgunīnakṣatramaryamā devatā..... .... .... anurādhā nakṣatraṃ mitro devatā .... ..... .... .....*) (Tai. Saṃ. 4-4-9-2) that is, it is said that Aryamā is the lord of the Pūrva Phalgunī constellation and Mitra the lord of the Anurādhā constellation.
Aryamā:—In the sūktas addressed to the Ādityas, by the Ādityas are generally meant Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā. As we stated before, in the Ṛgveda (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1) only the names of six persons are given: Mitra, Aryamā, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa. In the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-114-3 the number of the Ādityas is seven, and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-22-8 it is said that Aditi first presented seven sons to the gods and afterwards obtained the eighth, Mārtāṇḍa. **NOTE** In A. Ve. 8-9-21 and in Tai. Brā. 1-1-9-1 it is said that Aditi had eight sons: Mitra, Aryamā, Varuṇa, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Dhātṛ, Indra and Vivasvān. **NOTE** In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (Śa. Brā. 6-1-2-8; 11-6-3-8) it is said that the Ādityas are twelve, one for each month. In the Purāṇas and the like also the Ādityas are twelve.

### Printed p. 55

Although the name of the deity Aryamā occurs in the Ṛgveda in about a hundred places, because he has no complete personality, Yāska, in his Nirukta (in the Nighaṇṭu), when explaining the derivation of the names of the deities, has left out the name of this deity Aryamā. Except in two mantras, in all other places he is mentioned only along with other deities, and that mostly along with Mitra and Varuṇa. Addressing Agni, when winning maidens,
त्वमर्यमा भवसि यत्कनीनां नाम स्वधावन् गुह्यं बिभर्षि (*tvamaryamā bhavasi yatkanīnāṃ nāma svadhāvan guhyaṃ bibharṣi*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3)
it is said that you are Aryamā.
भगो आर्यमा सविता पुरंधिर्मह्यं त्वादुर्गार्हपत्याय देवाः । (*bhago āryamā savitā puraṃdhirmahyaṃ tvādurgārhapatyāya devāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-85-36)
In this Ṛk the bridegroom says: O gods, Bhaga, Aryamā, Savitṛ and Purandhi gave me this bride to help me in my life as a householder.
'न तमंहो न दुरितं देवासो अष्ट मर्त्यं । सजोषसो यमर्यमा मित्रो नयंति वरुणो अति द्विषः ॥ (*'na tamaṃho na duritaṃ devāso aṣṭa martyaṃ | sajoṣaso yamaryamā mitro nayaṃti varuṇo ati dviṣaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-126-1)
शुनमस्मभ्यमूतये वरुणो मित्रो आर्यमा । शर्म यच्छंतु सप्रथ आदित्यासो यदीमहे अति द्विषः ॥ (*śunamasmabhyamūtaye varuṇo mitro āryamā | śarma yacchaṃtu sapratha ādityāso yadīmahe ati dviṣaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-126-7)
In the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-126, although the deities are said to be the Viśvedevas, it is chiefly the Ādityas who are praised here by the Ṛks quoted above. Besides, देवासः अष्ट (*devāsaḥ aṣṭa*) in the first Ṛk refers to the eight Ādityas (nowhere is the number of the Vedic Ādityas said to be more than eight). Besides, the word आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*) in the 7th Ṛk also shows this clearly. In every Ṛk of this sūkta (except the last Ṛk) the names of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा (*varuṇo mitro aryamā*), are recited again and again like a refrain; this indicates the principal place these three, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryamā, hold among the Ādityas. Since the subject of Aryamā is not described at length in the Ṛgveda, a special explanation is not possible.

### Printed p. 56

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒प्तभिः॑ पु॒त्रैरदि॑ति॒रुप॒ प्रैत्ू॒र्व्यं यु॒गं ।  
> प्र॒जायै॑ मृ॒त्यवे॑ त्व॒त्पुन॑र्मार्तां॒डमाभ॑रत् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सप्तभिः । पुत्रैः । अदितिः । उप । प्र । ऐत् । पूर्व्यं । युगं ।  
> प्रजायै । मृत्यवे । त्वत् । पुनः । मार्तांडं । आ । अभरत् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पूर्वमंत्रोक्त एवार्थः पुनरत्रोच्यते । सप्तभिर्मातांडव्यतिरिक्त्कैर्मित्रादिभिरदितिः पूर्व्यं पुराणं युगमुप प्रैत् । उपगता । अथ प्रजायै प्राणिनामुत्पत्तये मृत्यवे तेषां मरणाय मार्तांडं मृताद्वृद्धादंडाज्ञातं
> मार्तांडनामानं सूर्यं पुनराभरत् । आहरत् । द्युलोकेऽधारयत् । प्राणिमरणजननादीनां सूर्योदयास्तमयायत्तता स्पुटा । तस्यै वृद्धमांडमजायतेत्यादि ब्राह्मणं । तै-सं. ६-५-६-१ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The meaning already stated in the previous mantra is stated here again. With the seven, Mitra and the others, other than Mārtāṇḍa, Aditi went to the former, ancient age (yuga). Then, for the birth of creatures and for their death, she brought back Mārtāṇḍa, the Sun, so called because he was born from the dead or old egg; she established him in the heavenly world. That the birth and death of creatures depend on the rising and setting of the Sun is plain. The Brāhmaṇa says तस्यै वृद्धमांडमजायतेत्यादि (*tasyai vṛddhamāṃḍamajāyatetyādi*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-5-6-1) **GLOSS**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अदिति: (*aditi:*)—Aditi
- सप्तभिः पुत्रैः (*saptabhiḥ putraiḥ*)—with seven sons
- पूर्व्यं युगं (*pūrvyaṃ yugaṃ*)—to the former age
- उप प्रैत् (*upa prait*)—went and joined
- पुनः: (*punaḥ:*)—moreover
- त्वत् मार्तांडं (*tvat mārtāṃḍaṃ*)—the (second) Sun called Mārtāṇḍa
- प्रजायै (*prajāyai*)—for the origin of creatures
- मृत्यवे (*mṛtyave*)—and for their decay
- आभरत् (*ābharat*)—established in the heavenly world

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Aditi went and joined the former age with seven sons. The remaining eighth Āditya she established in the heavenly world, both for the origin of creatures and for their decay.

##### English translation
With seven sons Aditi went to a former generation, but she bore Martanda for the birth and death (of human beings).

### Printed p. 57

The seventy-third sūkta.

## Sūkta 73 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> जनिष्ठा इत्येकादशर्चं पंचमं सूक्तं शक्तिपुत्रस्य गौरिवीतेरार्षं मारुतं
> त्रैष्टुभं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । जनिष्ठा एकादश गौरिवीतिरिति ॥ अग्निष्टोमे मरुत्वतीयशस्त्र इदं सूक्तं निविद्धानं । सूत्रितं च । जनिष्ठा उग्र इत्येकभूयसीः शस्त्रा
> मरुत्वतीयां निविदं दध्यात् । आ. ५-१४ । इति ॥ सौमिकचातुर्मास्येषु वैश्वदेवेऽप्येतन्मरुत्वतीयं निविद्धानं । सूत्रितं च । जनिष्ठा उग्र उग्रो जज्ञ इति माध्यंदिनः । आ. ९-२ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽपि निष्केवल्य एतत्तूक्तं । तथैव पंचमारण्यके
> सूत्रितं । जनिष्ठा उग्रः सहसे तुरायेति निविद्धानं । ऐ. आ. ५-१-१ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning जनिष्ठा (*janiṣṭhā*) has eleven Ṛks and is the fifth sūkta; the seer is Gauriviti, son of Śakti; the deity is the Maruts; the metre is Triṣṭubh. So it is stated in the Anukramaṇī: जनिष्ठा एकादश गौरिवीतिरिति (*janiṣṭhā ekādaśa gaurivītiriti*). In the Agniṣṭoma, in the Marutvatīya śastra, this sūkta is used as the nivid-dhāna. It is stated in the Sūtra: जनिष्ठा उग्र इत्येकभूयसीः शस्त्रा मरुत्वतीयां निविदं दध्यात् (*janiṣṭhā ugra ityekabhūyasīḥ śastrā marutvatīyāṃ nividaṃ dadhyāt*) (Ā. 5-14). In the Soma-sacrifices called Cāturmāsya, in the Vaiśvadeva also, this Marutvatīya is the nivid-dhāna. It is stated: जनिष्ठा उग्र उग्रो जज्ञ इति माध्यंदिनः (*janiṣṭhā ugra ugro jajña iti mādhyaṃdinaḥ*) (Ā. 9-2). In the Mahāvrata also this sūkta is used in the Niṣkevalya. Likewise it is stated in the fifth Āraṇyaka: जनिष्ठा उग्रः सहसे तुरायेति निविद्धानं (*janiṣṭhā ugraḥ sahase turāyeti nividdhānaṃ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-1).

Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning जनिष्ठाः (*janiṣṭhāḥ*) is the fifth sūkta in the sixth Anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gauriviti, son of the seer Śakti; the deity is the Maruts; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said जनिष्ठा एकादश गौरिवीतिः (*janiṣṭhā ekādaśa gaurivītiḥ*). When the Marutvatīya śastra mantras are recited in the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, this sūkta is to be recited as the nivid-dhāna sūkta; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra जनिष्ठा उग्र इत्येकभूयसीः शस्त्वा मरुत्वतीयां निविदं दध्यात् (*janiṣṭhā ugra ityekabhūyasīḥ śastvā marutvatīyāṃ nividaṃ dadhyāt*) (Ā. 5-14). In the sacrifices called Cāturmāsya, connected with Soma, at the time of reciting the Vaiśvadeva śastra mantras, this sūkta is to be recited as the Marutvatīya nivid-dhāna sūkta; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra जनिष्ठा उग्र उग्रो जज्ञ इति माध्यंदिनः (*janiṣṭhā ugra ugro jajña iti mādhyaṃdinaḥ*) (Ā. 9-2). In the Mahāvrata sacrifice also, the use of this sūkta for the Niṣkevalya śastra mantras is indicated in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence जनिष्ठा उग्रः सहसे तुरायेति निविद्वानं (*janiṣṭhā ugraḥ sahase turāyeti nividvānaṃ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-1).

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 73.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 3, 4.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11.
Seer: गौरिवीतिः (*gaurivītiḥ*).
Deity: इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) (मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)) **NOTE**
Metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*).

### Printed p. 58
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> जनि॑ष्ठा उ॒ग्रः सह॑से तु॒राय॑ मं॒द्र ओजि॑ष्ठो बहु॒लाभि॑मानः ।  
> अव॑र्ध॒न्निंद्रं॑ म॒रुत॑श्चि॒दत्र॑ मा॒ता यद्वी॒रं द॒धन॒द्धनि॑ष्ठा ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जनिष्ठाः । उग्रः । सहसे । तुराय । मंद्रः । ओजिष्ठः । बहुलऽअभिमानः ।  
> अवर्धन् । इंद्रं । मरुतः । चित् । अत्र । माता । यत् । वीरं । दधनत् । धनिष्ठा li

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र सहसे बलाय तुराय शत्रूणां हिंसनाय त्वमुग्र उद्धूर्णबलो जनिष्ठाः । अजायथाः । कीदृशस्वं । मंद्रः स्तुत्य ओजिष्ठः । ओजः शरीरबलं ।
> अतिशयेन तद्वान् बहुलाभिमानो भूयिष्ठाभिमानी। ईदृशं महानुभावमिंद्र.
> मत्र वृत्रवधे मरुतश्चिन्मरुतोऽप्यवर्धन् । स्तुत्या साहाय्येन वा वर्धितवंतः ।
> यद्यदा धनिष्ठा धारयित्रिंद्रमाता वीरं दधनत् आधारयत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, for strength (सहसे (*sahase*)) and for the slaying of enemies (तुराय (*turāya*)) you were born (जनिष्ठाः (*janiṣṭhāḥ*)), fierce, that is, of surging strength. What kind are you? Adorable (मंद्रः (*maṃdraḥ*)), most mighty (ओजिष्ठः (*ojiṣṭhaḥ*); ओजः (*ojaḥ*) is bodily strength, and you have it in the highest degree), and extremely proud (बहुलाभिमानः (*bahulābhimānaḥ*)), that is, greatly self-confident. Such a mighty Indra the Maruts also strengthened here, at the slaying of Vṛtra, by their praise or by their help. This was when (यत् (*yat*)) the sustaining mother (धनिष्ठा (*dhaniṣṭhā*), the one who bears Indra) sustained (दधनत् (*dadhanat*)) the hero."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बहुलाभिमानः (*bahulābhimānaḥ*)—one who has great self-confidence (O Indra)
- मंद्रः (*maṃdraḥ*)—worthy of praise
- ओजिष्ठः (*ojiṣṭhaḥ*)—endowed with vigour; you
- सहसे (*sahase*)—for a display of strength
- तुराय (*turāya*)—for the destruction of enemies
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—possessing exceeding strength
- जनिष्ठाः (*janiṣṭhāḥ*)—you have been born
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- धनिष्ठामाता (*dhaniṣṭhāmātā*)—your mother who bore Indra
- वीरं (*vīraṃ*)—you, the valiant
- दधनत् (*dadhanat*)—had borne, then
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—you, the lord
- अत्र (*atra*)—at this time of the slaying of Vṛtra
- मरुतश्चित् (*marutaścit*)—the Maruts also
- अवर्धन् (*avardhan*)—strengthened

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who have great self-confidence, who are worthy of praise and endowed with vigour, have been born with exceeding strength for a display of strength and for the destruction of enemies. When your mother bore you, the valiant, then at the time of the slaying of Vṛtra the Maruts also praised you, the lord, and strengthened you.

### Printed p. 59

##### English translation
You have been born, (Indra), for strength, for slaying (enemies), powerful, adorable, most mighty, extremely proud; the Maruts animated Indra in the Vritra-fight) when (his) sustaining mother sustained the hero.
#### Ṛk 2
**NOTE**

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> द्रु॒हो निष॑त्ता पृश॒नी चि॒देवैः॑ पु॒रू शंसे॑न वावृधु॒ष्ट  ॑
> इंद्रं ।  
> अ॒भीवृ॑तेव॒ ता म॑हाप॒देन॑ ध्वां॒तात्प॑पि॒त्वा मद॑रंत॒ गर्भाः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> द्रुहः । निसत्ता । पृशनी । चित् । एवैः । पुरु । शंसेन । ववृधुः । ते । इंद्रं ।  
> अभिवृताइव । ता । सुहापदेन । ध्वांतात् । प्रपित्वात् । उत् । अरंत । गर्भाः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्रुहो द्रोग्गुरिंद्रस्य पृशनी चित् सेनापि निषत्ता । तत्संनिधौ निषण्णा भूत् ।
> एवैर्गंतृभिर्मरुद्भिः सहितमिंद्रं निषण्णेति संबंधः । तेऽपि मरुतः पुरु प्रभूतेन शंसेन स्तोत्रेणेंद्रं ववृधुः । अवर्धंत वृत्रं जिघांसंतं । अथ महापदेन
> महता व्रजेनाभीवृतेव परिवृतानीव गवादीनि तानि यथावरणापगमे निर्गच्छंति
> तद्वृत्तान्न्युदकानि ध्वांतादंधकाररूपात्पित्वादासन्नात्प्राप्ताद्वृत्राद्गर्भा गर्भभूतान्न्युदकान्युदरंत । उदगच्छन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The army (पृशनी चित् (*pṛśanī cit*)) of Indra, the destroyer of enemies (द्रुहः (*druhaḥ*)), also sat down (निषत्ता (*niṣattā*)), that is, took its seat near him. The construction is that it sat around Indra, who was accompanied by the Maruts, the goers (एवैः (*evaiḥ*)). Those Maruts also strengthened (ववृधुः (*vavṛdhuḥ*)) Indra with abundant (पुरु (*puru*)) praise (शंसेन (*śaṃsena*)), when he wished to slay Vṛtra. Then, as cattle and the like that have been enclosed within a great stall (महापदेन (*mahāpadena*)) come out when the enclosure is removed, so the waters that were embryo-like within Vṛtra, who was near, in the form of darkness (ध्वांतात् (*dhvāṃtāt*)), rose up (उदरंत (*udaraṃta*)) and went out.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्रुहः (*druhaḥ*)—of Indra, the destroyer of enemies
- पृशनी चित् (*pṛśanī cit*)—the army also
- एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—surrounding Indra, who was accompanied by the Maruts
- निषत्ता (*niṣattā*)—sat down
- ते (*te*)—those Maruts also
- पुरु (*puru*)—abundant
- शंसेन (*śaṃsena*)—with praise
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- ववृधुः (*vavṛdhuḥ*)—strengthened (and thereafter)
- महापदेन (*mahāpadena*)—by the walls of a great cattle-pen
- अभीवृतेव (*abhīvṛteva*)—as cattle that have been surrounded and shut in run out together in a herd as soon as a gate is found

### Printed p. 60
- ध्वांतात् (*dhvāṃtāt*)—from Vṛtra, who is of the form of darkness
- प्रपित्वात् (*prapitvāt*)—who had surrounded the waters, and who was fattened
- ता गर्भाः (*tā garbhāḥ*)—the waters that were contained within him
- उदरंत (*udaraṃta*)—went out and flowed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The army of Indra, the destroyer of enemies, surrounded Indra, who was accompanied by the Maruts, and took its place to help him. The Maruts also praised Indra abundantly and strengthened him. Straightway, as cattle that have been surrounded by the walls of a great cattle-pen and shut in run out together in a herd as soon as the gate is found, the waters, which were like an embryo within Vṛtra, who is of the form of darkness, who had surrounded the waters and who was fattened, went out and flowed.

##### English translation
The martial troop of (Indra) the injurer encamped around Indra, (accompanied) by the swift moving (Maruts): they animated him with abundant praise ; like (cattle) penned up within a great stall, the embryonic (waters) issued from the (Vritra) who had arrived in the form of darkness.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒ष्वा ते॒ पादा॒ प्र यज्जिगा॒स्यव॑र्ध॒न्वाजा॑ उ॒तये चि॒दत्र॑ ।  
> त्वमिं॑द्र सालावृ॒कान्स॒हस्र॑मा॒सन्न॑धिषे अ॒श्विना न॑वृत्याः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋष्वा । ते । पादा । प्र । यत् । जिगासि । अवर्धन् । वाजाः । उत । ये । चित् ।  
> अत्र ।  
> त्वं । इंद्र । सालावृकान् । सहस्रं । आसन् । दधिषे । अश्विना । आ । ववृत्याः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते पादावृष्टा महांतौ । तादृशस्त्वं यद्यदा जिगासि गच्छसि
> तदानीं वाजा ऋभवोऽवर्धन् । अवर्धयन् । उतापि च ये चिद्ये केचिद्देवाः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, your feet are vast (ऋष्वा (*ṛṣvā*)), that is, great. When (यत् (*yat*)) you, being such, go (जिगासि (*jigāsi*)), then the Vājas (वाजाः (*vājāḥ*)), that is, the Ṛbhus, strengthened you; and also whatever (ये चित् (*ye cit*)) deities there are here, they too
### Printed p. 61

> संति तेऽप्यवर्धयन् । हे इंद्र त्वं सहस्रं सालावृकानासन्नास्यॆ दधिषे । धारयसि।
> तदानीमश्विनाश्विनावप्या ववृत्याः । आवर्तयेः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

strengthened you. O Indra, you hold a thousand jackals (सालावृकान् (*sālāvṛkān*)) in your mouth (आसन् (*āsan*), that is, in the mouth). Then you would bring back (आ ववृत्याः (*ā vavṛtyāḥ*), "you would cause to turn") the Aśvins also.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- पादा (*pādā*)—feet
- ऋष्वा (*ṛṣvā*)—are of great form
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- प्र जिगासि (*pra jigāsi*)—you move about, then
- वाजाः (*vājāḥ*)—the Ṛbhus
- उत (*uta*)—and
- ये चित् (*ye cit*)—all the other deities who are there
- आवर्धन् (*āvardhan*)—strengthen you
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—in the number of a thousand
- सालावृकान् (*sālāvṛkān*)—special animals
- आसन् (*āsan*)—in the mouth
- दधिषे (*dadhiṣe*)—you possess (at that time)
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—the Aśvin deities
- आ ववृत्याः (*ā vavṛtyāḥ*)—cause to return to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, your feet are of great form. When you move about well, the Ṛbhus and the other deities strengthen you. O Indra, you hold many special animals in your mouth. At that time cause the Aśvin deities to return to us.

##### English translation
Vast, (Indra), are your feet ; when you advance, the Vajas, and whatsoever (deities are) there animated you ; you, Indra, hold a thousand jackals in your mouth, may you bring back the Aswins.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒म॒ना तूर्णि॒रुप॑ यासि य॒ज्ञमा नास॑त्या स॒ख्याय॑ वक्षि ।  
> व॒साव्या॑मिंद्र धारयः स॒हस्रा॒श्विना॑ शूर ददत र्म॒घानि॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> समना । तूर्णिः । उप । यासि । यज्ञं । आ । नासत्या । सख्याय । वक्षि ।  
> वसाव्यां । इंद्र । धारयः । सहस्रा । अश्विना । शूर । दधतुः । मघानि ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 62

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र समना संग्रामे तूर्णिस्वरमाणोऽपि यज्ञमुप यासि । उपगच्छसि । तदानीं नासत्याश्विन् सख्याय वक्षि । वहसि । हे इंद्र वसाव्यां वसुसमूहं
> सहस्रा सहस्रां बहुसंख्यां धारयः । धारयस्यस्माकमर्थाय । हे शूर अश्विनाश्विनावपि तवानुचरौ मघानि धनानि ददतुः । अस्मभ्यं प्रयच्छतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, even while you hasten (तूर्णिः (*tūrṇiḥ*)) in battle (समना (*samanā*)) you come to (उप यासि (*upa yāsi*)) the sacrifice. Then you bring (वक्षि (*vakṣi*)) the Nāsatyas, the Aśvins, for friendship (सख्याय (*sakhyāya*)). O Indra, you hold (धारयः (*dhārayaḥ*)) for our sake a heap of treasures (वसाव्यां (*vasāvyāṃ*)) in thousands, that is, of great number. O hero, the Aśvins also, your followers, give (ददतुः (*dadatuḥ*)) riches (मघानि (*maghāni*)); they bestow them on us."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- समना (*samanā*)—in battle
- तूर्णिः (*tūrṇiḥ*)—even though you are engaged
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—to our sacrifice
- उपयासि (*upayāsi*)—you come
- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—the Aśvin deities
- सख्याय (*sakhyāya*)—to attain friendship with us
- वक्षि (*vakṣi*)—you bring to us
- वसव्यां (*vasavyāṃ*)—a heap of riches
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—in exceedingly great number
- धारयः (*dhārayaḥ*)—you hold
- शूर (*śūra*)—O Indra, the valiant
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—the Aśvin deities, who are yours, also
- मघानि (*maghāni*)—riches
- ददतुः (*dadatuḥ*)—give us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, even though you are engaged in battle, you come to our sacrifice. You bring the Aśvin deities to us, so that they may attain friendship with us. You possess a heap of riches in exceedingly great number. O valiant Indra, the Aśvin deities, who are yours, also give us riches.

##### English translation
Hastening in battle you approach the sacrifice; you bring the two Nasatyas to friendship (with us); you, Indra, possess a heap of treasures, thousands (in number) ; the Aswins, hero, gave us riches.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मंद॑मान ऋ॒तादधि॑ प्र॒जायै॒ सखि॑भि॒रिंद्र॑ इषि॒रेभि॒रर्थं॑ ।  
> आभि॒र्हि मा॒या उप॒ दस्यु॒मागा॒न्मिहः॒ प्र त॒म्रा अ॑वप॒त्तमां॑सि ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 63

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मंदमानः । ऋतात् । अधि । प्रजायै । सखिभिः । इंद्रः । इषिरेभिः । अर्थं ।  
> आ । अभिः । हि । मायाः । उप । दस्युं । आ । अगात् । मिहः । प्र । तम्राः । अवपत् ।  
> तमांसि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्र ऋताद्यज्ञादधीषिरेभिर्गमनशीलैः सखिभिर्मरुद्भिः सह मंदसानो
> माद्यन्मजाय्कॆ यजमानायार्थं धनं प्रयच्छति । स चेंद्र अभिः प्रजाभिर्निमित्तभूताभिर्माया दस्युसंबंधिनीर्विनाशयितुं दस्युमुपागात् । स दस्युस्तम्रा
> अवर्णणेन ग्ला पयित्रिर्मिहो वृष्टीः प्रावपत् । व्यनाशयदित्यर्थः । तमांसि .... ॥३॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, rejoicing (मंदसानो (*maṃdasāno*)) at the sacrifice (ऋतात् (*ṛtāt*)) together with his friends the Maruts, who are swift-going (इषिरेभिः (*iṣirebhiḥ*)), gives wealth (अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)) to the sacrificer. And that Indra, for the sake of destroying the illusions connected with the Dasyu, went (उपागात् (*upāgāt*)) to the Dasyu by means of these (अभिः (*abhiḥ*)) beings, who were the cause. That Dasyu, the one who afflicts (तम्राः (*tamrāḥ*)) through the lack of rain, scattered down (प्रावपत् (*prāvapat*)) the rains (मिहः (*mihaḥ*)); that is, Indra destroyed them. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- ऋतात् आधि (*ṛtāt ādhi*)—in the sacrificial place
- इषिरेभिः (*iṣirebhiḥ*)—swift-going
- सखिभिः (*sakhibhiḥ*)—with his friends, the Maruts
- मंदमानः (*maṃdamānaḥ*)—experiencing joy
- प्रजायै (*prajāyai*)—to the sacrificers
- अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)—gives wealth
- अभिः (*abhiḥ*)—with them
- मायाः (*māyāḥ*)—in order to destroy the evil power of the Asuras
- दस्युं (*dasyuṃ*)—to the Dasyu
- उपागात् (*upāgāt*)—comes
- तम्राः (*tamrāḥ*)—which afflicts the world through lack of rain
- मिहः (*mihaḥ*)—the condensed rain
- प्रावपत् (*prāvapat*)—made it melt and become rain
- तमांसि (*tamāṃsi*)—destroyed the darkness

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, experiencing joy in the sacrificial place with his friends the Maruts, gives wealth to the sacrificers. With them he comes to the Dasyu in order to destroy the evil power of the Asuras. He made the condensed rain, which afflicts the world through lack of rain, melt and pour down (makes it do so). He destroyed the darkness (destroys it).

##### English translation
Indra rejoicing at the sacrifice accompanied by his swift-moving friends (the Maruts, gave) wealth to the people; he has come with them against the Dasyu to destroy his illusions; he has scattered the dark rain-clouds and the gloom.

### Printed p. 64
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सना॑माना चिद्ध्वसयो॒ न्य॑स्मा॒ अवा॑ह॒न्निंद्र॑ उ॒षसो॒ यथानः॑ ।  
> ऋ॒ष्टैर॑गच्छः॒ सखि॑भि॒र्निका॑म्यैः सा॒कं प्र॑ति॒ष्ठा हृद्या॑ जघंथ ॥६॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सनामाना । चित् । ध्वसयः । नि । अस्मै । अव । अहन् । इंद्रः । उषसः । यथा ।  
> अनः ।  
> यष्टैः । अगच्छः । सखिभिः । निकाम्यैः । साकं । प्रतिस्था । हृद्या । जघंध ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रो वृत्रं हंतुं सनामाना चित् समाननामानौ नि ध्वसयः ।
> न्यगमयत् । अथेंद्रस्तं वृत्रमवाहन् । अवहतवान् । यथोषसोऽनः शकटमवनाशितवान् तद्वत् । अथ प्रत्यक्षेणोच्यते । हे इंद्र त्वमृष्टैर्दिप्तैर्महद्भिर्वा निकामैर्नित्तरां वृत्रवधं कामयमानैः सभिभिर्मरुद्भिः साकं वृत्रं हंतुमगच्छः ! आगत्य च प्रतिष्ठा प्रतिष्ठानानि शरीराणि हृद्या हृद्यानि रमणीयानि जघंथ ।
> हतवानसि ॥ हंतेस्थल्युपदेशेऽत्वत इतीट् प्रतिषेधॆः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, in order to slay Vṛtra, brought down (नि ध्वसयः (*ni dhvasayaḥ*), glossed न्यगमयत् (*nyagamayat*)) the two of the same name (सनामाना चित् (*sanāmānā cit*), glossed समाननामानौ (*samānanāmānau*)). Then Indra struck down (अवाहन् (*avāhan*), glossed अवहतवान् (*avahatavān*)) that Vṛtra, as the cart (अनः (*anaḥ*)) of Uṣas was destroyed. **NOTE** Now he is addressed directly: "O Indra, you went with the Maruts, who are shining (glossed as shining) or great, who are exceedingly eager (निकाम्यैः (*nikāmyaiḥ*)) for the slaying of Vṛtra, and who are your friends (सखिभिः (*sakhibhiḥ*)), to slay Vṛtra; and having come, you slew his supports (प्रतिष्ठा (*pratiṣṭhā*), glossed प्रतिष्ठानानि शरीराणि (*pratiṣṭhānāni śarīrāṇi*)), that is, his bodies, which were delightful (हृद्या (*hṛdyā*)), that is, pleasing."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स नामाना (*sa nāmānā*)—two Asuras of one name (O Indra)
- नि ध्वसयः (*ni dhvasayaḥ*)—you have destroyed
- यथा (*yathā*)—in the way
- उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)—of Uṣas
- अनः (*anaḥ*)—in which Vṛtra broke the cart, in that way
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—the Asura Vṛtra
- अवाहन् (*avāhan*)—threw far away
- ऋष्टै (*ṛṣṭai*)—radiant (O Indra)
- निकाम्यैः (*nikāmyaiḥ*)—who desired the slaying of Vṛtra
- सखिभिः साकं (*sakhibhiḥ sākaṃ*)—with the Maruts, who are your friends
- अगच्छः (*agacchaḥ*)—you pressed forward for the slaying of Vṛtra
- हृद्या (*hṛdyā*)—attractive
- प्रतिष्ठा (*pratiṣṭhā*)—the limbs of his body
- जघंथ (*jaghaṃtha*)—you have destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you have destroyed two Asuras of the same name. In the way in which Vṛtra broke the cart of Uṣas, in that way Indra threw the Asura Vṛtra far away. O Indra, you pressed forward for the slaying of Vṛtra with the Maruts, who are radiant, who desired the slaying of Vṛtra, and who are your friends. You have destroyed the attractive limbs of his body.

### Printed p. 65

##### English translation
You have expelled the two of the same name, you have destroyed the Vritra, as (he destroyed) the cart of the dawn; you advance with the mighty friends (the Maruts), who were eager (to slay the Vritra;) you demolished his precious limbs.
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं ज॑घंथ॒ नमु॑चिं मख॒स्युं दासं॑ कृण्वा॒न ऋष॑ये॒  ॑
> विमायं ।  
> त्वं च॑कर्थ॒ मन॑वे स्को॒नान्प॒थो दे॑व॒त्रांज॑सेव॒ याना॑न् ॥७॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । जघंथ । नमुचिं । मखस्युं । दासं । कृष्णानः । ऋषये । विमायं ।  
> त्वं । चकर्थ । मनवे । स्योनान् । पथः । देवत्रा । अंजसा इव । यानान् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं नमुचिमेतत्संज्ञकमसुरं जघंथ । हतनानसि । कीदृशं । मखस्युमृषेर्यज्ञं विधातुमिच्छंतं यद्वा त्वदीयं धनमिच्छंतं । किं कुर्वन् ।
> दासमुपक्षपयितारं नमुचिमसुरमृषये मनवे विमायं विगतमायं कृष्णानः
> कुर्वन् । किंच देवत्रा देवेषु मध्यॆ मनव ऋषये समानेन मनुष्याय वा
> पथो मार्गान्सोनांश्चकर्थ । कृतवानसि । अथवा देवत्रा देवेषु मध्यॆ गंतव्यान्मार्गानिति वा संबंधः । सोनांश्चकर्थेत्युक्तमेव विवृणोति । अंजसेव
> यानानिति । इवेत्येवकारार्थे । अंजसैवाकुटिलेनैव यानान्गंत्यन्मार्गानकरोः ।
> अथवा मार्गेणाकुटिलेनैव गंत्यंश्चकर्थ । यथा लोके शीघ्रं गतो मार्गः
> क्यांत्या गतो मार्ग इति मार्गस्य गंतृत्वोपचारः तद्वदत्रापि यातृत्वं द्रष्टव्यं ॥
> xxix 6

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you slew (जघंथ (*jaghaṃtha*), glossed हतवानसि (*hatavānasi*)) the Asura named Namuci. What kind of one? One who desired (मखस्युं (*makhasyuṃ*)) to perform the sacrifice of the seer, or else one who desired your wealth. What was he doing? Namuci, the Asura who was a servant (दासं (*dāsaṃ*), one who wastes away), was being made (कृण्वानः (*kṛṇvānaḥ*)) free of illusion (विमायं (*vimāyaṃ*), that is, with his illusion gone) for the seer, for Manu. Moreover, among the gods (देवत्रा (*devatrā*)) you made the paths pleasant (स्योनान् (*syonān*)) for Manu, the seer, or for man." Or else the construction is: paths to be travelled among the gods. Then he explains what was said, "you made the paths pleasant", by the words the phrase "like straight ways": "as if" is in the sense of "indeed"; you made the paths on which one goes (यानान् (*yānān*)) straight, not crooked. Or else, you made the travellers go by a straight road. As in the world one says "the road goes quickly" or "the road went in patience", attributing the quality of a traveller to the road, so also here the traveller-quality is to be seen. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 66

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you (O Indra)
- दासं (*dāsaṃ*)—the violent Namuci
- ऋषये (*ṛṣaye*)—for Manu
- विमायं (*vimāyaṃ*)—so that the deceit (of the Asura) does not appear
- कृण्वानः (*kṛṇvānaḥ*)—making
- नमुचिं (*namuciṃ*)—that Namuci
- जघंथ (*jaghaṃtha*)—you have destroyed
- किं च (*kiṃ ca*)—and
- देवत्रा (*devatrā*)—among the gods
- मनवे (*manave*)—for Manu
- पथः (*pathaḥ*)—the paths
- स्योनान् (*syonān*)—as pleasant ones
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you have made
- यानान् (*yānān*)—the paths of travel
- अंजसेव (*aṃjaseva*)—you have made straight ones also

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you destroyed the violent Namuci, breaking his deceitful nature, in order to bring benefit to Manu. And so that Manu may go and join the gods, you have made the path of the gods straight and beneficial.

##### English translation
You have slain the slave Namuchi endeavouring (to disturb) the sacrifice, making his illusions powerless against the Rishi; you have made easy for Manu the paths to the gods so as (to make) the ways straight.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नमुचिं (*namuciṃ*)—इंद्रेण सह युद्धं न मुंचतीति नमुचिः (*iṃdreṇa saha yuddhaṃ na muṃcatīti namuciḥ*): because he does not give up fighting with Indra, he is named Namuci. Namuci is an Asura. Since he was exceedingly strong, it was very difficult for Indra to fight him and conquer him. We have written about this Asura on some earlier occasions. But since the commentator indicates that the slaying of Namuci by Indra is told in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa, we have set it out here.

इंद्रो वृत्रग्ं हत्वा । असुरान् पराभाव्य । नमुचिमासुरं नालभत । तग्ं शच्यागृह्णात् । तौ समलभेतां सोऽस्मादभिशुनतरोऽभवत् । सोऽब्रवीत् । संधाग्ं संदधावहै । अथ त्वावस्रक्ष्यामि । न मा शुष्केण नाद्र्रेण हनः! न दिवा न नक्तमिति । स एतमपां फेनमसिंचत् । न वा विष शुष्को नार्द्रो वृष्टासीत् । अनुदितॆ: सूर्यः । न वा एतद्दिवा न नक्तं । तस्मैतस्मिन्लोके। अपां फेनेन शिर उदवर्तयत् । तदेनमन्ववर्तत । मित्रद्रुगिति । स एतानपामार्गानजनयत् । तानजुहोत् । तैर्वै स रक्षाग्स्यपाहत । यदपामार्गहोमो भवति । रक्षसामपहत्यै ॥ (*iṃdro vṛtragṃ hatvā | asurān parābhāvya | namucimāsuraṃ nālabhata | tagṃ śacyāgṛhṇāt | tau samalabhetāṃ so 'smādabhiśunataro 'bhavat | so 'bravīt | saṃdhāgṃ saṃdadhāvahai | atha tvāvasrakṣyāmi | na mā śuṣkeṇa nādrreṇa hanaḥ! na divā na naktamiti | sa etamapāṃ phenamasiṃcat | na vā viṣa śuṣko nārdro vṛṣṭāsīt | anuditè: sūryaḥ | na vā etaddivā na naktaṃ | tasmaitasminloke| apāṃ phenena śira udavartayat | tadenamanvavartata | mitradrugiti | sa etānapāmārgānajanayat | tānajuhot | tairvai sa rakṣāgsyapāhata | yadapāmārgahomo bhavati | rakṣasāmapahatyai ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-5, 6, 7)

### Printed p. 67
After Indra had slain the Asura Vṛtra, he slew still other demons. But he could not conquer the Asura named Namuci. He was exceedingly strong. He did not yield to any kind of weapons. Therefore Indra, wishing to conquer him by his bodily strength alone, began a wrestling-fight with him. But as the fight went on, Namuci's strength only increased. Indra could not escape from his grip. Then Indra asked Namuci to release him, and the Asura said to Indra: "O Indra, if I am to release you, you must do as I say. Let us now make a pact with each other. You may again wish to fight me by some stratagem and to kill me. In such a case the weapon that you use to kill me must be neither wet nor dry; you must not kill me by day, nor must you kill me by night. If you agree to these conditions, I shall release you." Indra agreed, saying "Let it be so," freed himself from his grip, and began to think how he should conquer him. After some time a means occurred to him. The foam of the sea, which was in front of him, fell under his eye. This foam is neither wet nor dry. "Therefore I shall make this my weapon and slay Namuci. When the night has passed and the dawn has begun, before the sun rises, it is quite light. That time is not night, because it is not dark, and it is not day, because the sun has not risen. If I fight at such a time and kill him with the weapon made of the foam of water, I shall not have broken the word I gave." So he decided, and in this way he fought with Namuci and cut off his head with that foam. Yet the Asura did not die, but pursued Indra in order to kill him: "O Indra, betrayer of a friend! Stop, stop! After promising that you would not kill me, you have now done such an act of treachery to a friend. I shall not let you go without killing you." Then Indra, frightened, created a special tree called Apāmārga and offered oblations in the fire with its fuel-sticks for the increase of his vigour. Through this Indra's strength grew greater and he slew that Namuci. Therefore it is said that those who wish to be freed from the affliction of demons should make oblations with Apāmārga fuel-sticks. The slaying of this Namuci by Indra is told, apart from this Ṛk, in the following Ṛks of the Ṛgveda:
नम्मा यदिंद्र सख्या परावति निबर्हयो नमुचिं नाम मायिनं । (*nammā yadiṃdra sakhyā parāvati nibarhayo namuciṃ nāma māyinaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7)
अत्रा दासस्य नमुचेः शिरो यदवर्तयो मनवे गातुमिच्छन् (*atrā dāsasya namuceḥ śiro yadavartayo manave gātumicchan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7)

### Printed p. 68
युजं हि मामकृथा आदिदिंद्र शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् (*yujaṃ hi māmakṛthā ādidiṃdra śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8)
प्र श्येनो न मदिरमंशुमस्मै शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् । (*pra śyeno na madiramaṃśumasmai śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-6)
निवेशने शततमाविवेषीरहञ्च वृत्रं नमुचिमुताहन् (*niveśane śatatamāviveṣīrahañca vṛtraṃ namucimutāhan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5)
अपां फेनेन नमुचेः शिर इंद्रोदवर्तयः (*apāṃ phenena namuceḥ śira iṃdrodavartayaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-13)
यः पिपुं नमुचिं यो रुधिक्रां तस्मा इंद्रायांधसो जुहोत । (*yaḥ pipuṃ namuciṃ yo rudhikrāṃ tasmā iṃdrāyāṃdhaso juhota |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-5)
युवं सुराममश्विना नमुचावासुरे सचा (*yuvaṃ surāmamaśvinā namucāvāsure sacā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4)
It is told in these Ṛks and nowhere else.
मनवे (*manave*)—we have explained the matter of Manu in Part 18, pages 178-179.
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वमे॒तानि॑ षप्रिषे॒ वि नामेशा॑न इंद्र दधिषे॒ गभ॑स्तौ ।  
> अनु॑ त्वा दे॒वाः शव॑सा मदंत्यु॒परि॑ बुधान्व॒निन॑ श कथ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)
