In this Ṛk the seer has addressed the waters with आहः (*āhaḥ*) **NOTE**, "O waters". But are the waters, which are lifeless things, endowed with consciousness? May they be regarded like human beings who have consciousness, or like gods? On this matter Yāska has given a special explanation—
अथाकारचिंतनं देवतानां! पुरुषविधाः स्तुरित्येकं। चेतनावद्वद्धि स्तुतयोः भवंति । तथाभिधानाति । अथापि पुरुषविधिकैरंगैः संस्तूयंते । ऋष्वात इंद्र स्थविरस्य बाहू । यत्संगृभ्णामघवन्काशिरित्ते । अथापि पौरुषविधिकैर्द्रव्यसंयोगैः । आ द्वाभ्यां हरिभ्यामिंद्र याहि कल्याणीर्जाया सुरणं गृहे ते । अथापि पौरुषविधिकैः कर्मभिः । अद्दींद्र पिब च प्रस्थितस्य । आ श्रुत्कर्ण श्रुधी हवं ॥ (*athākāraciṃtanaṃ devatānāṃ! puruṣavidhāḥ sturityekaṃ| cetanāvadvaddhi stutayoḥ bhavaṃti | tathābhidhānāti | athāpi puruṣavidhikairaṃgaiḥ saṃstūyaṃte | ṛṣvāta iṃdra sthavirasya bāhū | yatsaṃgṛbhṇāmaghavankāśiritte | athāpi pauruṣavidhikairdravyasaṃyogaiḥ | ā dvābhyāṃ haribhyāmiṃdra yāhi kalyāṇīrjāyā suraṇaṃ gṛhe te | athāpi pauruṣavidhikaiḥ karmabhiḥ | addīṃdra piba ca prasthitasya | ā śrutkarṇa śrudhī havaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 7-6)
When the seers praise the gods, they attribute to them form and the other things that human beings have, and praise them accordingly. Although human beings cannot know that the forms of the gods are just so
### Printed p. 101
by seeing them, it is natural to suppose that they also have hands, feet and other organs, as human beings have, who are seen by the eye every day. In the same way, attributing to the gods the limbs and other things that human beings have, the seers have praised them. In the passage ऋष्टात इंद्र स्थविरस्य बाहू यत्संगृध्नामघवन्माशिरित्ते (*ṛṣṭāta iṃdra sthavirasya bāhū yatsaṃgṛdhnāmaghavanmāśiritte*) the meaning is: O Indra, the fist of your strong arms holds the earth and the atmosphere firmly; this shows that Indra has arms and a fist as human beings have. In some other places it is said that the gods, like men, have the power of possessing wealth (property). In आ द्वाभ्यां हरिभ्यामिंद्र याहि कल्याणीर्जाया सुरणं गृहे ते (*ā dvābhyāṃ haribhyāmiṃdra yāhi kalyāṇīrjāyā suraṇaṃ gṛhe te*) there is mention of Indra's wife and house. In still other places the actions that human beings perform are attributed to the gods; in the sentences अद्दींद्र पिब च प्रस्थितस्य । आ श्रुत्कर्ण श्रुधी हवं (*addīṃdra piba ca prasthitasya | ā śrutkarṇa śrudhī havaṃ*) there is mention of actions such as eating, drinking and hearing: O Indra, eat the oblation prepared here, drink the Soma, hear our invocation with your ear.
Some others say as follows—
अपुरुषविधाः स्युरित्यपरं । अपि तु यद्धृश्यतेs पुरुषविधं तद्यथाग्निर्वायु रादित्यः पृथिवी चंद्रमा इति । यथो एतच्छेतनावद्वद्धि स्तुतयो भवंतीत्यचेतनान्यस्येवं स्तूयंते यथाक्षप्रभृतिन्नोषधिपर्यंतानि । यथो एततत्पौरुषविधिकैरंगैः संस्तूयंत इत्यचेतनेष्वष्टेतद्भवत्यभिक्रंदति हरितेभिरासभिरिति ग्रावस्तुतिः । यथो एतत्पौरुषविधिकैर्द्रव्यसंयोगैरित्येतदपि तादृशमेव । सुखं रथं युयुजे सिंधुरश्वनं इति नदीस्तुतिः ! यथो एतत्पौरुषविधिकैः कर्मभिरित्येतदपि तादृशमेव । होतुश्चित्तूर्वे हविरद्यमाशत इति ग्रावस्तुतिरेव । अपि वोभयविधाः स्युः । (*apuruṣavidhāḥ syurityaparaṃ | api tu yaddhṛśyates puruṣavidhaṃ tadyathāgnirvāyu rādityaḥ pṛthivī caṃdramā iti | yatho etacchetanāvadvaddhi stutayo bhavaṃtītyacetanānyasyevaṃ stūyaṃte yathākṣaprabhṛtinnoṣadhiparyaṃtāni | yatho etatatpauruṣavidhikairaṃgaiḥ saṃstūyaṃta ityacetaneṣvaṣṭetadbhavatyabhikraṃdati haritebhirāsabhiriti grāvastutiḥ | yatho etatpauruṣavidhikairdravyasaṃyogairityetadapi tādṛśameva | sukhaṃ rathaṃ yuyuje siṃdhuraśvanaṃ iti nadīstutiḥ ! yatho etatpauruṣavidhikaiḥ karmabhirityetadapi tādṛśameva | hotuścittūrve haviradyamāśata iti grāvastutireva | api vobhayavidhāḥ syuḥ |*) (Ni. 7-4)
Others again say as follows. The gods may have no limbs, actions and so on such as human beings have, but since their actions and the like are seen by the eye, limbs and other things such as human beings have are attributed to those gods and they are praised. There is no fault in this. For example, fire, wind, the sun, the earth and so on are seen by our eyes, and their actions, burning, shining and so on, are within experience, so it must be said that the attribution of limbs and actions to those gods is natural. In some other cases it may be necessary to speak in this way even of lifeless things, as of the dice of gambling, the herbs and trees, the rivers, the stones that crush the Soma juice and other lifeless things, to which consciousness is attributed and which are praised as being like human beings
### Printed p. 102
engaging in actions. In the Śruti sentence सुखं रथं युयुजे सिंधुरश्विनं (*sukhaṃ rathaṃ yuyuje siṃdhuraśvinaṃ*) the river Sindhu is praised by describing her as having yoked a chariot. In the sentence अभिक्रंदंति हरितेभिरासभीः (*abhikraṃdaṃti haritebhirāsabhīḥ*) the stones that crush the Soma (the green colour of whose faces (mouths) is produced by crushing the Soma plants) cry out with their green mouths; here also the action of making a sound, as human beings speak with the mouth, is attributed to the stones and they are praised.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ते॑ऽरद॒द्वरु॑णो॒ यात॑वे प॒थः सिंधो॒ यद्वाजा॑ अ॒भ्यद्र॑व॒स्त्वं ।  
> भूम्या॒ अधि॑ प्र॒वता॑ यासि॒ सानु॑ना॒ यदे॑षा॒मग्रं॒ जग॑तामिर॒ज्यसॆ ॥ २ ॥॑

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ते । अरदत् । वरुणः । यातवे । पथः । सिंधो इति । यत् । वाजान् । अभि ।  
> अद्रवः । त्वं ।  
> भूम्याः । अधि । प्रवता । यासि । सानुना । यत् । एषां । अग्रं । जगतां ।  
> इरज्यसि ॥ २ ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सिंधो देवि ते तव यातने गमनाय वरुणो देवः पथो
> मार्गान्पारदत् । प्राचीनं व्यलिखत् । सा हि प्राक् प्रवहति । अथवा प्रकृष्टमितरनदीभ्योऽपत्यंतं विस्तृतं व्यदारयत् । यद्यस्माद्दे सिंधो वाजानन्नान्यभिलक्ष
> त्वमभ्यद्रवः अभ्यगच्छः । किंच त्वं भूम्या अध्युपरि सानुना समुच्च्रितेन प्रवता
> मार्गेण यासि । गच्छसि । सिंधुः खलु पर्वतान्विभिद्य गता । यद्येन समुच्च्रितेन
> मार्गेण गच्छंती त्वमेषां सर्वेषां जगतां जंगमानां प्राणिनामग्रं
> प्रत्यक्षमिरज्यसि ईशिषे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O goddess Sindhu, for your going Varuṇa the god has made the paths (पथः (*pathaḥ*), glossed मार्गान् (*mārgān*)): अरदत् (*aradat*) is glossed प्राचीनं व्यलिखत् (*prācīnaṃ vyalikhat*), "he drew it eastward", for she flows eastward. Or else, प्रकृष्टमितरनदीभ्योऽपत्यंतं विस्तृतं व्यदारयत् (*prakṛṣṭamitaranadībhyo 'patyaṃtaṃ vistṛtaṃ vyadārayat*): he split it open, extending it exceedingly beyond the other rivers. Because you, O Sindhu, have gone towards food (वाजान् (*vājān*) is glossed as food, अभ्यद्रवः (*abhyadravaḥ*) as "you went towards"), and further you go over the earth by a lofty road (सानुना (*sānunā*) and प्रवता (*pravatā*) are glossed as "raised up" and "road"), the Sindhu has indeed gone splitting the mountains. By which lofty road, going, you rule (इरज्यसि (*irajyasi*), glossed ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)) in the sight of all these moving beings, that is, living creatures.
### Printed p. 103

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधो (*siṃdho*)—O river Sindhu
- यत् (*yat*)—for which reason
- वाजान् (*vājān*)—looking to food
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- अभ्यद्रवः (*abhyadravaḥ*)—you move swiftly, and therefore
- ते (*te*)—your
- यातवे (*yātave*)—for easy movement
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuṇa
- पथः (*pathaḥ*)—the paths
- प्रारदत् (*prāradat*)—has split open and set right
- यत् (*yat*)—in which lofty region
- एषां जगतां (*eṣāṃ jagatāṃ*)—to all these beings
- अग्रं (*agraṃ*)—so as to be manifest
- इरज्यसि (*irajyasi*)—you obtain lordship, such
- भूम्याः अधि (*bhūmyāḥ adhi*)—upon the earth
- सानुना (*sānunā*)—in the foothill region
- प्रवता (*pravatā*)—in the sloping part
- यासि (*yāsi*)—you flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O river Sindhu, for what reason you move swiftly towards food, for that very reason, for your easy movement, the god Varuṇa has split open and set right the paths. Upon the earth, in the foothill region, in the sloping part, in the lofty region where you obtain lordship so as to be manifest to all these beings, you flow.

##### English translation
For your course, Sindhu, Varuna tore open a path, since you hastened towards food ; you go by a lofty road down upon the earth, by which (road) you reign in the sight of all worlds.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

O river Sindhu, you flow in the steep region of lofty mountains and the like. Varuṇa has made for you, by splitting the mountains and the like, such a difficult path along which you flow, so that you may flow easily. Because you flow, your waters become the cause of the growth of plants, and by that means supply food and other nourishment to all beings. Thus it is for the sake of supplying food and the like to beings, that is, for the good of the world, that you flow. Therefore you are the lord of all: this is the chief purport of this Ṛk.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वि स्व॒नो य॑तते॒ भूम्यो॒पर्य॑नं॒तं शुष्म॒मुदि॑यर्ति  ॒॑
> भा॒नुना॑ ।

### Printed p. 104

> अभ्रादिव प्र स्तनयंति वृष्टयः सिंधुर्यदेति वृषभो न
> रोरुवत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवि। स्वनः। यतते । भूम्या । उपरि । अनंतं। शुष्मं । उत् । इयर्ति । भानुना ।  
> अभ्रात्ऽइव । प्र । स्तनयंति । वृष्टयः । सिंधुः । यत् । एति । वृषभः । न । रोरुवत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भूम्योपरि भूमेरुपरि प्रवर्तमानः स्वनो दिवि यतते । गच्छति । दिवं
> प्राप्नोति । सेयं सिंधुरनंतमपर्यंतं शुष्मं वेगं भानुना दीप्तेनोर्मिणोदियर्ति । उद्गमयति । अभ्रादिवांतरिक्षाद्यथा वृष्टयः प्र स्तनयंति तद्वदस्याः
> शब्दाः प्रादुर्भवंति । यद्यदा सिंधुरियं रोरुवद्भृशं शब्दयन् वृषभो न
> वृषभ इवैति गच्छति तद्वैवं भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sound (स्वनः (*svanaḥ*)) that arises over the earth goes to heaven (दिवि यतते (*divi yatate*), glossed गच्छति (*gacchati*) and दिवं प्राप्नोति (*divaṃ prāpnoti*)), that is, it reaches heaven. This Sindhu sends up (उदियर्ति (*udiyarti*), glossed उद्गमयति (*udgamayati*)) her endless, limitless force (शुष्मं (*śuṣmaṃ*), glossed वेगं (*vegaṃ*), "speed") by her shining wave (भानुना (*bhānunā*), "shining wave"). As rains thunder forth from the cloud, that is, from the atmosphere, so her sounds arise. When this Sindhu goes roaring loudly, making a great noise like a bull, then it is so.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भूम्याः (*bhūmyāḥ*)—of the earth
- उपरि (*upari*)—in the upper part, manifested
- स्वनः (*svanaḥ*)—the sound
- दिवि (*divi*)—to the upper atmosphere
- यतते (*yatate*)—spreads and goes (O Sindhu)
- अनंतं (*anaṃtaṃ*)—endless
- शुष्कं (*śuṣkaṃ*)—your speed
- भानुना (*bhānunā*)—in the form of brilliance
- उदियर्ति (*udiyarti*)—you impel and spread
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- सिंधुः (*siṃdhuḥ*)—the Sindhu
- वृषभो न (*vṛṣabho na*)—like a bull
- रोरुवत् (*roruvat*)—making a noise
- एति (*eti*)—flows, then
- अभ्रात् (*abhrāt*)—from the cloud that is in the atmosphere
- वृष्टय इव (*vṛṣṭaya iva*)—as rains fall with a sound
- प्र स्तनयंति (*pra stanayaṃti*)—the flood of the Sindhu flows with a sound

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sound of the river, manifested on the upper part of the earth, spreads and goes to the upper atmosphere. O Sindhu, you impel and spread your endless speed in the form of brilliance. When the Sindhu flows making a noise like a bull, then, as rain falls with a sound from the cloud that is in the atmosphere, the flood of the Sindhu flows with a sound.

##### English translation
The sound goes forth in heaven above the earth; (Sindhu) with shining wave animates his endless speed ; as rains issue thundering from the cloud, so Sindhu, (thunders) when it advances roaring like a bull.
### Printed p. 105

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the speed with which the river Sindhu flows and the sound it makes as it flows are described. The roaring sound of the flood is compared with the sound of heavy rain.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि त्वा॑ सिंधो॒ शिशु॒मिन्न मा॒तरो॑ वा॒श्रा अ॑र्षंति॒ पय॑सेव  ॒॑
> धेनवः ।  
> राजे॑व॒ युध्वा॑ नयसि॒ त्वमित्सिचौ॒ यदा॑सा॒मग्रं॑ प्र॒वता॒मिन॑क्षसि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । त्वा । सिंधो इति । शिशुं । इत् । न । मातरः । वाश्राः । अर्षंति । पयसा इव ।  
> धेनवः ।  
> राजा इव । युध्वा । नयसि । त्वं । इत् । सिचौ । यत् । आसां । अग्रं । प्रवतां ।  
> इनक्षसि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सिंधो त्वा त्वां शिशुमिन्न मातरः पुत्रमिव वाश्राः शब्दयंत्य इतरा
> नद्योऽभ्यर्षंति । अभिगच्छंति । पयसेव धेनवः पयसा युक्ता नवप्रसूतिका
> गाव इव । किंच युध्वा युद्धकृद्राजीव त्वमित्त्वमेव सिचौ सिच्यमानौ तटा
> नयस्युदकपूरं यद्यदासां प्रवतां त्वया सह गच्छंतीनामग्रमग्र इनक्षसि
> व्याप्नोषि सर्वासां पुरतो गच्छसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Sindhu, the other rivers, sounding (वाश्राः (*vāśrāḥ*), "sounding"), come towards you (अभ्यर्षंति (*abhyarṣaṃti*), glossed अभिगच्छंति (*abhigacchaṃti*)) as mothers come to a child, and as cows (धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)) newly calved and full of milk come to their calves. Further, like a king who goes to battle (युध्वा (*yudhvā*), "one who makes war"), you alone lead the two banks (सिचौ (*sicau*), "the two banks being drenched") to fullness of water **NOTE**. When (यत् (*yat*), glossed यदा (*yadā*)) you pervade (इनक्षसि (*inakṣasi*), glossed व्याप्नोषि (*vyāpnoṣi*)) the front of these flowing rivers that go with you, that is, when you go before all of them, then it is so.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधो (*siṃdho*)—O Sindhu
- मातरः (*mātaraḥ*)—mothers
- शिशुमिन्न (*śiśuminna*)—as they call and cry out to their sons
- वाश्राः (*vāśrāḥ*)—the other rivers that make a sound
- पयसा (*payasā*)—filled with milk
- धेनव इव (*dhenava iva*)—like cows
- त्वा अभ्यर्षंति (*tvā abhyarṣaṃti*)—are coming towards you
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- आसां प्रवतां (*āsāṃ pravatāṃ*)—of the other rivers that flow with you
- अग्रं (*agraṃ*)—in front
- इनक्षसि (*inakṣasi*)—you flow, pervading
### Printed p. 106
when you flow, then
- युद्धा (*yuddhā*)—going out for battle
- राजीव (*rājīva*)—like a king
- सिचौ (*sicau*)—the drenched banks
- नयसि (*nayasi*)—you cross and press forward

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Sindhu, as mothers affectionately call and cry out to their sons, the other tributaries that make a sound come towards you like cows full of milk. When you flow as the chief of the other rivers that flow with you, then, like a king, you cross the two banks and flow on.

##### English translation
Like mothers crying for their sons, (the other rivers) hasten toward you, Sindhu, like milch cows with their milk ; you lead your two wings like a king going to battle when you march in the van of the streams that are descending (with you).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the tributaries are mentioned. As cows that have just calved go with affection, lowing, to their calves, so your tributaries, as they flow, join you with a sound. You are the chief of all these rivers, and, overflowing your banks with their waters, you flow like the march of a king who fights: this is the chief purport.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं मे॑ गंगे यमुने सरस्वति॒ शुतु॑द्रि॒ स्तोमं॑ सचता॒  ॒
> परु॒ष्ण्या ।  
> अ॒सि॒क्न्या म॑रुद्वधे वि॒तस्त॒यार्ज॑कीये शृणु॒ह्या सु॒षोम॑या ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमं । मे । गंगे । यमुने । सरस्वति । शुतुद्रि । स्तोमं । सचत । परुष्ठि । आ  
> असिक्ना । मरुत् वृधे । वितस्तया । अर्जीकीये । शृणुहि । आ । सुसोमया ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 107

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्र प्रधानभूताः सप्त नद्यस्तदवयवभूता नद्यस्ति स्रः स्तूयंते । हे गंगे
> हे यमुने हे सरस्वति हे शुतुद्रि हे परुष्ठि । हे असिक्न्यावयवभूतया सहिते
> मरुद्वधे हे वितस्तया सुषोमया च सहित आर्जीकीये त्वं च एवं सप्त नद्यः
> यूयं मे स्तोमं स्तोत्रमस्मदीयमा सचत । आसेवध्वं । शृणुहि । शृणुत ।
> आर्जीकीयाया वितस्तया सुषोमया च साहित्यं निरुक्त उक्तं । वितस्तया
> चार्जीकीय आ शृणुहि सुषोमया चेति । अत्र गंगा गमनादित्यादि, निरुक्तं
> द्रष्टव्यं । ९-२६ । ॥ ६ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Here the seven principal rivers and the three rivers that are their constituent parts are praised. O Gaṅgā, O Yamunā, O Sarasvatī, O Śutudri, O Paruṣṭhi; O Marudvṛdhā, accompanied by Asiknī, which is her constituent; and O Ārjīkīyā, accompanied by Vitastā and Susomā; and you: thus, seven rivers, you attend my praise, my song of praise (आ सचत (*ā sacata*), glossed आसेवध्वं (*āsevadhvaṃ*)); hear it (शृणुहि (*śṛṇuhi*), glossed as the plural, "hear"). The association of Ārjīkīyā with Vitastā and Susomā is stated in the Nirukta: चार्जीकीय आ शृणुहि सुषोमया चेति (*cārjīkīya ā śṛṇuhi suṣomayā ceti*). Here, for गंगा गमनादित्यादि (*gaṃgā gamanādityādi*) and the rest, the Nirukta is to be consulted, Ni. 9-26. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गंगे (*gaṃge*)—O river Gaṅgā
- यमुने (*yamune*)—O Yamunā
- सरस्वति (*sarasvati*)—O Sarasvatī
- शुतुद्रि (*śutudri*)—O Śutudri
- परुष्ठि (*paruṣṭhi*)—O Paruṣṭhi
- असिक्ना (*asiknā*)—joined with the river Asiknī
- मरुद्वृधे (*marudvṛdhe*)—O river Marudvṛdhā
- वितस्तया (*vitastayā*)—and with the river Vitastā
- सुषोमया (*suṣomayā*)—joined also with the river Susomā
- आर्जिकीये (*ārjikīye*)—O river Ārjikā
- मे (*me*)—recited by me
- इमं स्तोमं (*imaṃ stomaṃ*)—this praise
- आ शृणुत (*ā śṛṇuta*)—hear
- आ सचत (*ā sacata*)—attend

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Gaṅgā, O Yamunā, O Sarasvatī, O Śutudri, O Paruṣṭhi, O Marudvṛdhā joined with Asiknī, O Ārjikā joined with Vitastā and with the river Susomā, hear this praise recited by me, and attend it.

##### English translation
Accept this my praise, Ganga, Yamuna, Sarasvati, Shutudri, Parushni, Marudvridha with Asikni and Vitasta ; listen, Arjikiya with Sushoma.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Along with the names of these seven chief rivers, the names of some more rivers or tributaries are also stated. Ten river names are stated here: Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Sarasvatī, Śutudri, Paruṣṭhi, Asiknī, Marudvṛdhā, Vitastā, Ārjikī and Susomā. Yāska has given the Nirukta for this Ṛk—
### Printed p. 108
इमं मे गंगे यमुने सरस्वति शुतुद्रि परुष्ठि स्तोममासेवध्वमसिक्ना च सह मरुद्वृधे वितस्तया चार्जीकीय आशृणुहि सुषोमया चेति समस्तार्थः ॥ (*imaṃ me gaṃge yamune sarasvati śutudri paruṣṭhi stomamāsevadhvamasiknā ca saha marudvṛdhe vitastayā cārjīkīya āśṛṇuhi suṣomayā ceti samastārthaḥ ‖*)
अथैकपदनिरुक्तं—गंगा गमनात् ॥ यमुना प्रयुवती गच्छतीति वा प्रवियुतं गच्छतीति वा ॥ सरस्वती सर इत्युदकनाम सर्तॆस्तद्वती ॥ शुतुद्रीशुद्राविणी क्षिप्रद्राविण्याशु तुन्नेव द्रवतीति वा ॥ इरावतीं परुष्ठित्याहुः—पर्ववती भास्वती कुटिलगामिनी ॥ असिक्ष्य शुक्ला सिता—सितमिति वर्णनाम तत्पतिषेधोऽसितं ॥ मरुद्वधाः सर्वा नद्योमरुत एना वर्धयंति वितस्ता विदग्धा विवृद्धा महाकूला ॥ आर्जिकीयां विपाडित्याहुः । ख.जीकप्रभवा वर्जुगामिनी वा विपाड्विपाट. नाद्वा विप्राशनाद्दा विप्रापणाद्दा पाशा अस्यां व्यपाश्यंत वसिष्ठस्य मुमूर्षतस्तस्माद्विपाडुच्यते ॥ पूर्वमासीदुरुंजिरा ॥ सुषोमा सिंधुर्यदेनामभिप्रसुवंति नद्यः ॥ सिंधुः स्यंदनात् ॥ (*athaikapadaniruktaṃ—gaṃgā gamanāt ‖ yamunā prayuvatī gacchatīti vā praviyutaṃ gacchatīti vā ‖ sarasvatī sara ityudakanāma sartèstadvatī ‖ śutudrīśudrāviṇī kṣipradrāviṇyāśu tunneva dravatīti vā ‖ irāvatīṃ paruṣṭhityāhuḥ—parvavatī bhāsvatī kuṭilagāminī ‖ asikṣya śuklā sitā—sitamiti varṇanāma tatpatiṣedho 'sitaṃ ‖ marudvadhāḥ sarvā nadyomaruta enā vardhayaṃti vitastā vidagdhā vivṛddhā mahākūlā ‖ ārjikīyāṃ vipāḍityāhuḥ | kha.jīkaprabhavā varjugāminī vā vipāḍvipāṭa. nādvā viprāśanāddā viprāpaṇāddā pāśā asyāṃ vyapāśyaṃta vasiṣṭhasya mumūrṣatastasmādvipāḍucyate ‖ pūrvamāsīduruṃjirā ‖ suṣomā siṃdhuryadenāmabhiprasuvaṃti nadyaḥ ‖ siṃdhuḥ syaṃdanāt ‖*) (Ni. 9-26)
The total meaning of this Ṛk is: O Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Sarasvatī, Śutudri joined with Paruṣṭi, Marudvṛdhā who is with Āsiki, Ārjikīyā who is with Vitastā, all of you hear this praise that I am reciting **NOTE**. For each word, Gaṅgā and so on, the Nirukta, that is, the meaning of the parts, will be stated—
गंगा (*gaṃgā*)—the word Gaṅgā is formed from a word having the sense of going, formed from the root गम्लृ (*gamlṛ*), which has the meaning "go" (flow).
यमुना (*yamunā*)—this river is called Yamunā because it flows mingled with (joined to) other rivers, or because it flows slowly or without sound.
सरस्वती (*sarasvatī*)—सरः (*saraḥ*) means water. Having water means having much water, or, flowing (moving on) without stopping; therefore this river is called Sarasvatī.
शुतुद्रि (*śutudri*)—because it runs swiftly, or flows swiftly, or flows swiftly as if running at the urging of others, this river is called Śutudri.
### Printed p. 109
परुष्टि (*paruṣṭi*)—the river Irāvatī itself is called the river Paruṣṭi. परुष्टि (*paruṣṭi*) means having joints or knots. Because this river flows in a winding course, the name Paruṣṭi has come to this river.
असिक्नी (*asiknī*)—it means not white, being dark. सितं (*sitaṃ*) means white. असित (*asita*) means not white, being dark. Because the water of this river is not clear but has a slightly dark shade, the name Asiknī has come to this river.
मरुद्वधे (*marudvadhe*)—because this river grows and flows in flood from all the other rivers (its tributaries) and from the wind, it is called Marudvṛdhā.
वितस्ता (*vitastā*)—it means a river with high banks made of unburnt, that is, raw sticky earth.
आर्जीकीया (*ārjīkīyā*)—this is also called विपाड् (*vipāḍ*). Because this river is born and flows in the country called ऋजुक (*ṛjuka*), or because it flows straight without bends (turns), this river is called Ārjīkīyā.
विपाट् (*vipāṭ*)—because this river, as it flows, overflows its banks, or because it flows beyond the two banks that confine it, or because it spreads widely and flows beyond the banks, this river is called Vipāṭ. In former times the sons of the seer Vasiṣṭha died, and the seer, unable to bear the grief for his sons, wished to take his own life; he bound his hands and feet with cords and plunged into this river Vipāṭ. But the river, not wishing to kill him, tore off his cords and saved his life. Because it tore off the cords in this way, the name Vipāṭ came to this river. In former times this river had the name उरुंजिरा (*uruṃjirā*), a river with a great deal of water.
सुषोमा (*suṣomā*)—this river is also called the river Sindhu. For all the rivers (its tributaries) flow to it, in order to join it (for confluence). The word सिंधु (*siṃdhu*) means flowing. Some are of the opinion that the name Susomā (सु (*su*) + सोम (*soma*)) came to this river because excellent Soma plants grew on its bank.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तृ॒ष्णाम॑या प्रथ॒मं यात॑ने स॒जूः सु॒सर्ता॑ र॒सया॑ श्वे॒त्वा  
> त्या

### Printed p. 110

> त्वं सिंधो कुभया गोमतीं क्रुमुं मेहत्वा सरथं
> याभिरीयसे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तृप्तऽअमया । प्रथमं । यातवे । सजूः । सुसत्वा । रसया । श्वेत्या । त्या ।  
> त्वं । सिंधो इति । कुभया । गोमतीं । क्रुमुं। मेहत्वा । सरथं । याभिः ।  
> ईयसे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सिंधो त्वं क्रुमुं क्रमणशीलां गोमतीं नदीं यातवे प्रतियातुं
> पर्वतादवरूढा प्रथमं तृष्टामया नद्या सजूः संगतासि । पश्चात्सुसर्त्वा रसया
> श्वेत्या त्या कुभया मेहत्ना च सह सजूर्भव याभिस्तृं सरथं समानं
> रथमारुह्येयसे । गच्छसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Sindhu, you, descending from the mountain in order to go to meet (यातवे (*yātave*), glossed प्रतियातुं (*pratiyātuṃ*)) the Gomatī river, which is "going" (क्रुमुं (*krumuṃ*), glossed क्रमणशीलां (*kramaṇaśīlāṃ*)), first united (सजूः (*sajūḥ*), glossed संगतासि (*saṃgatāsi*)) with the river Tṛṣṭāmā **NOTE**. Afterwards be united with Susartu, Rasā, Śvetī, त्या (*tyā*), Kubhā and Mehatnu, with which you go mounting one and the same chariot (सरथं (*sarathaṃ*), glossed "the same chariot").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधो (*siṃdho*)—O river Sindhu
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- क्रुमुं (*krumuṃ*)—flowing
- गोमतीं (*gomatīṃ*)—with the river Gomatī
- यातने (*yātane*)—to go and join
- प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*)—first
- तृष्णामया (*tṛṣṇāmayā*)—with the river Tṛṣṇāmā
- सजूः (*sajūḥ*)—you have joined (and)
- याभिः (*yābhiḥ*)—with which rivers
- सरथं (*sarathaṃ*)—mounting the same chariot
- ईयसे (*īyase*)—you go, such rivers
- सुसर्त्वा (*susartvā*)—with Susartu
- रसया (*rasayā*)—with Rasā
- श्वेत्या (*śvetyā*)—with Śvetā
- कुभया (*kubhayā*)—with Kubhā
- त्या (*tyā*)—famous
- मेहत्वा (*mehatvā*)—with Mehantu, joining, flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O river Sindhu, in order to go and join the flowing river Gomatī you have first united with the river Tṛṣṇāmā. And in the same way flow on, joined also with these rivers, Susartu, Rasā, Śvetā, Kubhā and Mehantu, who are fit to sit in the same chariot with you.

##### English translation
You, Sindhu, in order to reach to swift-moving Gomati, have united yourself first with the Trishtama : (now be united) wfth the Susartu, the Rasa, the Shweti, the Kubha, and the Mehatnu, in conjunction with which streams you advance.
### Printed p. 111

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the names of the tributaries of the river Sindhu, the rivers Gomatī, Tṛṣṇāmā, Susartu, Rasā, Śvetī, Kubhā and Mehantu, are stated. The name of the river Rasā is indicated in the 108th sūkta of this maṇḍala; we have mentioned it in the introduction to this sūkta. Nothing is known about the other rivers.
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋजी॒त्येनी॒ रुश॑ती महि॒त्वा परि॒ ज्रयां॑सि भरते॒ रजां॑सि ।  
> अद॑ब्धा॒ सिंधु॑र॒पसा॑म॒पस्त॒माश्वा॒ न चि॒त्रा वपु॑षीव दर्श॒ता ॥७॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋजीती । एनी । रुशती । महित्वा । परि । ज्रयांसि । चरते । रजांसि ।  
> अदब्धा । सिंधुः । अपसां । अपर्तमा । अश्वा । न । चित्रा । वपुषी इव । दर्शता ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋजीत्यृजुगामिन्येनी श्वेतवर्णा रुशती दीप्यमाना सिंधुर्ज्रयांसि
> वेगवंति रजांस्युदकानि परि भरते । प्रभरति । तादृश्यदब्धाहिंसिता सिंधुरपसां
> कर्मवतीनां मध्येऽपस्तमा वेगाख्यकर्मवती भवति । अश्वा न वडवेव चित्रा
> चायनीया वपुषी वपुष्मती योषिदिव दर्शता दर्शनीया भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Ṛjītī is the one who goes straight (one who goes in a straight course), Enī is the white-coloured one, Rusatī is the shining one. The Sindhu carries forward (परि भरते (*pari bharate*), glossed प्रभरति (*prabharati*)) swift (ज्रयांसि (*jrayāṃsi*), glossed वेगवंति (*vegavaṃti*)) waters (रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*), glossed "waters"). Being such, unharmed (अदब्धा (*adabdhā*), glossed हिंसिता (*hiṃsitā*)), the Sindhu is, among the active rivers (अपसां (*apasāṃ*), glossed कर्मवतीनां (*karmavatīnāṃ*)), the most active (अपस्तमा (*apastamā*)), active in the work called speed. Like a mare (अश्वा न (*aśvā na*), glossed वडवेव (*vaḍaveva*)) she is wonderful and fit to be looked at (चित्रा (*citrā*), glossed चायनीया (*cāyanīyā*)); like a woman with a beautiful body (वपुषी (*vapuṣī*), glossed वपुष्मती योषिदिव (*vapuṣmatī yoṣidiva*)) she is beautiful to see (दर्शता (*darśatā*), glossed दर्शनीया (*darśanīyā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋजीती (*ṛjītī*)—she who has a straight course
- एनी (*enī*)—she who is white in colour
- रुशती (*ruśatī*)—she who is shining, the Sindhu
- महित्वा (*mahitvā*)—by her greatness
- जयांसि (*jayāṃsi*)—having swift floods
- रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*)—the waters
- परि भरते (*pari bharate*)—she bears
- अदब्धा (*adabdhā*)—unharmed
- सिंधुः (*siṃdhuḥ*)—the Sindhu
- अपसां (*apasāṃ*)—among the rivers whose action is that of a flood
- अपस्तमा (*apastamā*)—she is one whose action is the greatest
- अश्वा न (*aśvā na*)—like a horse
- चित्रा (*citrā*)—and attractive
- वपुषी इव (*vapuṣī iva*)—like a woman who has a beautiful body
- दर्शता (*darśatā*)—she is also one of beautiful form

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Sindhu, who goes straight, who is of clear colour and shining, bears by her greatness waters with swift floods. The unharmed Sindhu is, among the rivers whose action is that of a flood,
### Printed p. 112
one whose action is the greatest. She is attractive like a mare, and fit to be looked at like a woman with a beautiful body.

##### English translation
Straight-flowing, white-coloured, bright-shining (Sindhu) bears along in its might the rapid waters; the inviolable Sindhu, the most efficacious, is speckled like a mare, beautiful as a handsome woman.
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्वश्वा॒ सिंधुः॑ सु॒रथा॑ सु॒वासा॑ हिर॒ण्ययि॒ सुकृ॑ता वा॒जिनी॑वती ।  
> ऊर्णा॑वती युव॒तिः सी॒लमा॑वत्यु॒ताधि॑ वस्ते सु॒भगा॑ मधु॒वृधं॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुऽअश्वा । सिंधुः । सुरथा । सुऽनासाः । हिरण्यया । सुकृता । वाजिनीवती ।  
> ऊर्णावती । युवतिः । सीलमावती । उत । अधि । वस्ते । सुभगा । मधुवृधं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सेयं सिंधुः स्वश्वा शोभनाश्चोपेता सुरथा शोभनरथा सुवासाः
> शोभनवस्रोपेता हिरण्यया हिरण्मयाभरणा सुकृता वाजिनीवत्यन्नवत्यूर्णावती ।
> तस्याः समीपदेशे हि संत्यूर्णा यासां रोमभिः कंबलाः क्रियंते । युवतिर्नित्यतरुणी सर्वदाहीनोदका सीलमावती । सीराणि ययौषध्या रज्जु भूतया बध्यंते
> सा सीलमेति निगद्यते कृषीवलैः । तादृगोषध्युपेता । उतापि च सुभगा सिंधुर्मधु.
> वृधं मधुवर्धकं निर्गुंड्यादिकमधि वस्ते । अच्छादयति । तस्यास्तीरे निर्गुंड्यादीनि
> बहूनि संति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Sindhu is स्वश्वा (*svaśvā*), endowed with good horses; सुरथा (*surathā*), having a good chariot; सुवासाः (*suvāsāḥ*), having good clothing; हिरण्यया (*hiraṇyayā*), having golden ornaments; सुकृता (*sukṛtā*); वाजिनीवती (*vājinīvatī*), rich in food; ऊर्णावती (*ūrṇāvatī*), having wool: in the region near her there are sheep, from whose hair blankets are made. She is युवतिः (*yuvatiḥ*), ever young, one who never lacks water; सीलमावती (*sīlamāvatī*): the plant with which ploughs are bound as with a rope is called सीलमा (*sīlamā*) by farmers, and she is endowed with that plant. And further, the fortunate Sindhu wears (अधि वस्ते (*adhi vaste*), glossed अच्छादयति (*acchādayati*), "covers") मधुवृधं (*madhuvṛdhaṃ*), that which increases honey, nirguṇḍī and the like; on her bank there are many such plants as nirguṇḍī.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधुः (*siṃdhuḥ*)—the river Sindhu
- स्वश्वा (*svaśvā*)—endowed with excellent horses
- सुरथा (*surathā*)—having excellent chariots
- सुवासाः (*suvāsāḥ*)—having excellent garments
- सुकृता (*sukṛtā*)—having excellent
### Printed p. 113
actions
- हिरण्यया (*hiraṇyayā*)—having golden ornaments
- वाजिनीवती (*vājinīvatī*)—rich in food
- ऊण्रावती (*ūṇrāvatī*)—having wool
- युवतिः (*yuvatiḥ*)—having youth
- शीलमावती (*śīlamāvatī*)—having plants with the fibre called śīlamā; she is such
- उत (*uta*)—and
- सुभगा (*subhagā*)—the Sindhu, who is auspicious
- मधुवृधं (*madhuvṛdhaṃ*)—flowers that give honey in abundance
- अधिवस्ते (*adhivaste*)—wears

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The river Sindhu has excellent horses, chariots, garments and actions, golden ornaments, is rich in food, has regions that give wool, has youth, and has plants with the fibre called śīlamā. And the auspicious Sindhu wears flowers that give honey in abundance.

##### English translation
The Sindhu, is rich in horses, rich in chariots, rich in clothes, rich in gold ornaments, well-made, rich in food, rich in wool, ever fresh, abounding in Silama plants, and the auspieious river wears honey-growing (flowers).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Here the river Sindhu is praised by attributing to her horses, chariots and the like, as human beings have them. In this matter see the Special Topics we have written for the first Ṛk of this sūkta.
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒खं रथं॑ युयुजे॒ सिंधु॑र॒श्विनं॒ तेन॒ वाजं॑ सनिषद॒स्मिन्ना॒जौ।  
> म॒हान्ह॑स्य महि॒मा प॑न॒स्यतेऽद॑ब्धस्य॒ स्वय॑शसो विर॒प्सिनः॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुखं । रथं । युयुजे । सिंधुः । अश्विनं । तेन । वाजं । सनिषत् । अस्मिन् । अजौ ।  
> महान् । हि । अस्य । महिमा । पनस्यते । अदब्धस्य । स्वयशसः । विरप्यनः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

### Printed p. 114

> सिंधुर्देवता सुखं सुखकरं शोभनद्वारं वाश्विनमश्ववंतं रथं
> युयुजे । युनक्ति । तेन रथेन वाजमन्नं सनिषत् : प्रयच्छतु । अस्मिन्नाजौ
> संग्रामे यज्ञे वास्य सिंधुरथस्य महिमा महान्नि पनस्यते । स्तूयते ।
> कीदृशस्यास्य । अदब्धस्यान्यैरहिंस्यस्य स्वयशसः स्वायत्तकीर्तेर्विरप्पिनः महन्नामैतत् । महतः । ॥ ७ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The deity Sindhu has yoked (युयुजे (*yuyuje*), glossed युनक्ति (*yunakti*)) a pleasant (सुखं (*sukhaṃ*), glossed सुखकरं शोभनद्वारं (*sukhakaraṃ śobhanadvāraṃ*), "making for comfort, having a handsome opening") chariot with horses (अश्विनं (*aśvinaṃ*), "with horses"). With that chariot may she supply (सनिषत् (*saniṣat*), glossed प्रयच्छतु (*prayacchatu*)) food (वाजं (*vājaṃ*), "food"). In this battle or sacrifice (अस्मिन्नाजौ (*asminnājau*), glossed संग्रामे यज्ञे वा (*saṃgrāme yajñe vā*)) the greatness of this Sindhu's chariot is praised (पनस्यते (*panasyate*), glossed स्तूयते (*stūyate*)) as great. Of what kind is this chariot? Unharmed by others (अदब्धस्य (*adabdhasya*)), of self-won fame (स्वयशसः (*svayaśasaḥ*), "fame dependent on himself"), विरप्पिनः (*virappinaḥ*); he adds महन्नामैतत् (*mahannāmaitat*), "this is a name of the great". **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधु (*siṃdhu*)—the deity Sindhu
- सुखं (*sukhaṃ*)—pleasant
- अश्विनं (*aśvinaṃ*)—provided with horses
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- युयुजे (*yuyuje*)—has yoked
- तेन (*tena*)—with that same chariot
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food
- सनिषत् (*saniṣat*)—may she supply to us
- अस्मिन् आजौ (*asmin ājau*)—in this sacrifice
- आदब्धस्य (*ādabdhasya*)—unharmed
- स्वयशसः (*svayaśasaḥ*)—having fame won by itself
- विरप्पिनः (*virappinaḥ*)—having greatness
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this chariot of the deity Sindhu
- महिमा (*mahimā*)—the greatness
- महान् हि (*mahān hi*)—exceedingly
- पनस्यते (*panasyate*)—is praised

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The deity Sindhu has yoked a pleasant chariot with horses. In that same chariot may she supply food to us. The greatness of the chariot of the deity Sindhu, which is unharmed, has fame won by itself and has greatness, is exceedingly praised.

##### English translation
Sindhu has harnessed her easy-going well-horsed chariot, with it may she bring (us) food ; the might of this inviolable, great, renowned (chariot) at this sacrifice is praised as mighty.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Here it is said that the river Sindhu, or the deity, yoks her chariot. Is it not wrong to ascribe this power of action to a lifeless river? As the answer to this, we have discussed at length, in the Special Topics of the first Ṛk of this sūkta, the explanation that Yāska has given. One can see it there and understand.
### Printed p. 115
The seventy-sixth sūkta.
## Sūkta 76 (Maṇḍala 10)
**Introduction**
The deity of this sūkta is ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*). ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*) means the stones that are used for crushing the Soma plants and pressing out the juice. They have another name also, अद्रि (*adri*). These stones are praised not only in this sūkta but also in the 94th and the 175th sūktas of this same maṇḍala. For these three sūktas the stones themselves are the deity. In many other mantras there is a description of the praise of the stones. Besides regarding these stones with great reverence, there are many kinds of descriptions that the sound produced when the Soma plant is crushed with these stones is very sweet, that this sound is pleasing to Indra, and that Indra never failed to come to the place where that sound was. In the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28 the method of placing the Soma plant on the stone, crushing it and pressing out the juice is described in detail; therefore it will not be irrelevant to mention here the method of pressing the juice out of the Soma plants and other matters connected with it.
In the sacrifice, a stone with a broad base, used for crushing and pressing the Soma juice, and long enough to be convenient to hold in the hand, is lifted, and with it the Soma plant in the mortar is pounded and pressed and the juice is taken out.
We shall describe briefly here the procedure to be followed when the Soma juice is prepared. In the Soma sacrifices, for the drinking of Soma, the Soma plant is crushed and pressed, strained through a cloth, mixed according to the occasion with curds, milk and flour, offered to the gods, and then the institutor of the rite and the priests drink it themselves; this is the custom. This preparation of the Soma juice takes place on the principal day of the sacrifice in the morning, at midday and in the evening. It is called अभिवषण (*abhivaṣaṇa*) or सवन (*savana*). The rite of the morning is called प्रातःसवन (*prātaḥsavana*), the सवन (*savana*) performed at midday is called माध्यंदिनसवन (*mādhyaṃdinasavana*), and the सवन (*savana*) performed in the evening is called तृतीयसवन (*tṛtīyasavana*).
The Soma plant is not obtainable in the present day. Formerly it grew on the banks of rivers and on the mountains. It was brought with great effort, or bought from those who brought it, and used in the sacrifices; even now a rite called सोमविक्रयण (*somavikrayaṇa*) is performed in the sacrifices. The Soma plant (creeper) is of a बभ्रु (*babhru*) colour, आरुण (*āruṇa*) (dull red), or a red mixed with yellow. Since it is said that the creeper that grows very high on the mountains is the best and that more juice can be taken from it, in cases where it is possible the Soma plant was taken from the mountains themselves

### Printed p. 116

brought. Among such mountains the mountain called मूजवंत (*mūjavaṃta*) was famous. The shoot of this Soma plant is called by the name अंस (*aṃsa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-168-3; 3-48-2 and others); the shoot provided with sprouts is called वक्षणा (*vakṣaṇā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-1-17) and वाण (*vāṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-24-9; 9-50-1); the Soma juice is called अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28-7; 3-48-1; 4-16-1); and the stalk (creeper) of the Soma plant is called पर्वन् (*parvan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-9-1). Such is the usage.

The method of preparing the Soma juice is this: the leaves on the Soma plant, or on the creeper, are removed, the plant is put in a mortar and pounded with a stone. If the plant is not fresh, it is soaked in water for a day or two, and when it has become soft the juice is taken out. The mortar in which the Soma plant is pounded is called उलूखल (*ulūkhala*). It is generally made of stone; one made of wood also occurs. The stone that is put into the mortar and used to pound from above has the names ग्रावा (*grāvā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-83-6; 1-135-7) and आद्रि (*ādri*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-130-2; 1-135-5; 1-137-1). It may be supposed that the उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) was not like the mortar we use today, but only a slight hollow in the middle of a stone. This mortar called उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) was also used for pounding grain with the pestle (मुसल (*musala*)).

The vessel in which Soma is placed to be offered to the gods is called चमू (*camū*). The vessels that the officiating priests use for drinking Soma are called कलश (*kalaśa*) and चमस (*camasa*). In some contexts the word चमू (*camū*) is also used to denote the उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) and ग्रावा (*grāvā*) (the mortar and the pounding stone, mortar and pestle) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28-9; 4-18-3; 6-57-2).

When the Soma plants are pounded, it is the usual practice to place them on hides. This hide is called त्वक् (*tvak*). A cow-hide was used for this (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-94-9; 10-116-4). The vessels in which Soma is stored have names such as कोश (*kośa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-181-4; 8-20-8), सदस्य (*sadasya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-62-15; 9-1-2; 9-17-8), द्रु (*dru*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-1-2; 9-65-6; 9-98-2), वन (*vana*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-9; 9-66-9), द्रोण (*droṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-15-7; 9-33-2) and so on. स्रुव (*sruva*) means a ladle (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-24). The cloth or sieve with which the Soma juice is strained is called पवित्र (*pavitra*). In some cases Soma was also strained through the gaps between the fingers.

The Soma juice used without any admixture of other substances is called शुक्र (*śukra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-137-1; 3-32-2; 8-2-10) or शुचि (*śuci*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-5-5; 1-30-2; 8-2-9).

### Printed p. 117

The colour of this juice is described in various ways: it is said to be of various colours such as बभ्रु (*babhru*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-33-2; 9-63-4 and 6), हरित (*harita*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-3-9; 9-65-8), अरुण (*aruṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-40-2; 9-45-3), अरुष (*aruṣa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-61-21) and शोण (*śoṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-97-13), and to be fragrant (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-97.9; 9-107-2).

Certain substances are mixed with the Soma juice to give it taste. This mixture is called the आतिरद्रव्य (*ātiradravya*) substances. Soma juice mixed with milk is called गवाशिर (*gavāśira*), that mixed with curds दध्याशिर (*dadhyāśira*), and that mixed with flour यवाशिर (*yavāśira*). The Soma juice is sometimes said to be sharp; it is then to be understood as having a pungent smell or taste (pungent flavour). The husk-like part that remains after the Soma juice has been extracted is called ऋजीष (*ṛjīṣa*).

ग्रावा (*grāvā*)—the stone with which the Soma juice is pounded. As the word पृथुबुद्धः (*pṛthubuddhaḥ*) in this Ṛk explains, this stone is a round stone, broad at the bottom and like a handle at the top; a stone shaped like the grinding stone of a grinding-slab. In the Ṛgveda the stones used for pounding the Soma plant are generally called by the words ग्रावा (*grāvā*) and अद्रि (*adri*).

पृथुबुद्ध्न (*pṛthubuddhna*)—स्तुलमूल ऊर्ध्व उन्नतः (*stulamūla ūrdhva unnataḥ*). A long stone whose base is broad; the round stone that grinds on the slab. **NOTE**

ऊर्ध्वोभवति सोतवे (*ūrdhvobhavati sotave*)—for pounding the Soma plant the stone is lifted up with the hand. What is stated here is that, when the plant lying in the stone or in the mortar is to be pounded, another stone is held in the hand, lifted up and used for pounding.

उलूखलसुतानां (*ulūkhalasutānāṃ*)—उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) means a stone with a hollow in the middle, that is, a mortar. The stone has a hollow in the middle so that the juice does not run away when the Soma plant is pounded and the juice extracted. It is like a mortar. The Soma plants are put into this hollow part and pounded with another stone called ग्रावा (*grāvā*), and the juice is taken out. After the plant has been pounded, the juice stands in the hollow of the stone, just as the oil stands in the oil-press after sesame has been pressed. On the word उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) Yāska says:

उलूखलमुरुकरु वोर्ध्वखं वोर्क्करं वोरु मे कुर्वित्य ब्रवीत्तदुलूखलमभवत् । उरुकरं वैतत्तदुलूखलमित्याचक्षते परोक्षेणेति ब्राह्मणं ॥ (*ulūkhalamurukaru vordhvakhaṃ vorkkaraṃ voru me kurvitya bravīttadulūkhalamabhavat | urukaraṃ vaitattadulūkhalamityācakṣate parokṣeṇeti brāhmaṇaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 9.20). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 118

That is to say, in the word उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) one should put the letter ra in place of the letter la (since there is no distinction between la and ra); it then becomes उरुखरं (*urukharaṃ*) or उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*). Now उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*)—उरु बहु अथवा बह्वन्नं करोति उरुकरं (*uru bahu athavā bahvannaṃ karoti urukaraṃ*): (from pounding grain and the like in it) abundant food (nourishment) comes into being; because it produces what is abundant, it is called उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*). Or ऊर्ध्व (*ūrdhva*) + खं (*khaṃ*) = ऊर्ध्वखं (*ūrdhvakhaṃ*) = उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*); that is, because the grain is put into it and pounded by lifting the pestle upwards (from the root खन् (*khan*)), it is called उरुकर (*urukara*). Or ऊर्करं (*ūrkaraṃ*) = उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*); ऊर्क् (*ūrk*) means juice, and because it produces that (the Soma juice), that is, because in it the Soma plant is pounded and the Soma juice taken out, it is called ऊर्करं (*ūrkaraṃ*) or उरुकर (*urukara*). Or, when the sentence उरु मे कुरु इत्यब्रवीत् (*uru me kuru ityabravīt*) is uttered, it is called उरुकरु (*urukaru*) in accordance with its meaning.

Why must उरुकरं (*urukaraṃ*) be said to mean उलूखल (*ulūkhala*)? On this point there is the Brāhmaṇa sentence उरुकरं वैतत्तदुलूखलमित्याचक्षते (*urukaraṃ vaitattadulūkhalamityācakṣate*) परोक्षेणेति च ब्राह्मणं (*parokṣeṇeti ca brāhmaṇaṃ*), that is, that उरुकर (*urukara*) is the उलूखल (*ulūkhala*); this is cited here. In the present context the word उलूखलसुतानां (*ulūkhalasutānāṃ*) means: the portion of the Soma juice pounded in the mortar that is called उलूखल (*ulūkhala*). This उलूखल (*ulūkhala*) is one of the objects of sacrifice said to be the ten sacrificial weapons. On this matter:

यो वै दत यज्ञायुधानि वेद मुखतोऽस्य यज्ञः कल्पते स्पृश्च कपालानि चाग्निहोत्रहवणी च शूर्पं च कृष्णाजिनं च शम्या चोलूखलं च मुसलं च दृष्टच्चोपला चैतानि वै दश यज्ञायुधानि य एवं वेव मुजतोऽस्य यज्ञः कल्पते ॥ (*yo vai data yajñāyudhāni veda mukhato 'sya yajñaḥ kalpate spṛśca kapālāni cāgnihotrahavaṇī ca śūrpaṃ ca kṛṣṇājinaṃ ca śamyā colūkhalaṃ ca musalaṃ ca dṛṣṭaccopalā caitāni vai daśa yajñāyudhāni ya evaṃ veva mujato 'sya yajñaḥ kalpate ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-6-8)

This is a passage of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda; that is, the chief implements used in the sacrifice are ten:

- (1) स्पृः (*spṛḥ*)—च्छेदनाधिकृत् (*cchedanādhikṛt*). A knife used for cutting darbha grass and the like.
- (2) कपालानि (*kapālāni*)—हविरधिश्रयणार्थानि (*haviradhiśrayaṇārthāni*). The clay pans on which the oblation, that is, the oblation called पुरोडाश (*puroḍāśa*), is placed over the fire and cooked.
- (3) अग्निहोत्रहवणी (*agnihotrahavaṇī*)—a wooden vessel, like a ladle, used for pouring the oblation into the fire.
- (4) तूर्प (*tūrpa*)—a wooden implement used for winnowing grain.

### Printed p. 119

- (5) कृष्णाजिनं (*kṛṣṇājinaṃ*)—the skin of the antelope called कृष्णाजिन (*kṛṣṇājina*), used near the mortar.
- (6) शम्या (*śamyā*)—a weapon used in the sacrifice for killing the animal.
- (7) उलूखलं (*ulūkhalaṃ*)—the mortar used for pounding grain and for pounding the Soma juice.
- (8) मुसलं (*musalaṃ*)—the pestle for pounding grain.
- (9) दृष्ट् (*dṛṣṭ*)—the stone used at the bottom when Soma juice is pounded, or the grinding-slab. It is also called ग्रावा (*grāvā*).
- (10) उपला (*upalā*)—the round stone used for grinding what is placed on the stone called दृष्ट् (*dṛṣṭ*) mentioned above. It is also called ग्रावा (*grāvā*) or अद्रि (*adri*).

**NOTE**

Among these ten sacrificial implements is included the stone called ग्रावा (*grāvā*) with which Soma is pounded.

The method of pounding the Soma plant and preparing the Soma juice is nowhere described at length in the Ṛgveda. The method can be inferred from some sentences stated here and there. The stones with which the Soma plants are pounded have the names ग्रावा (*grāvā*) and अद्रि (*adri*). In some contexts wooden boards, well known by the name अधिषवण (*adhiṣavaṇa*), are also used. If the plant is fresh, much juice comes out. If not, that is, if some days have passed since it was cut and brought, it is soaked in water and then placed upon the hide. Then it is pounded with stones and the juice taken out. Since the juice so taken has the husks, fragments and the like of the plant mixed with it, it is strained either through the gaps between the fingers of the hands, or through the sieve or cloth called पवित्र (*pavitra*), or through the fleece of a sheep. The juice strained in this way is free from impurities and clear. It has the names शुक्र (*śukra*) and शुचि (*śuci*). To look at, this juice is of a dull red, or of a red mixed with yellow. It is the custom to offer to Indra and to Vāyu pure Soma juice, that is, Soma juice without any admixture of the आशिर (*āśira*) substances. For the other deities it is used mixed with milk, curds and the flour of grain. The Soma juice mixed with these has respectively the names गवाशिर (*gavāśira*), दध्याशिर (*dadhyāśira*) and यवाशिर (*yavāśira*).

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ व इत्यष्टर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं । इरावतः पुत्रस्य सर्पजातेर्जरत्कर्णनाम
> आर्ष० । जागतं । सोमाभिषवार्था ये ग्रावाणस्तद्देवत्यं । तथा चानुक्रांतं ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The eighth sūkta, beginning आ व (*ā va*), has eight Ṛks. Its seer is Jaratkarṇa, a serpent by birth, son of Irāvat; its metre is Jagatī; its deity is the Grāvans, the stones used for pressing Soma. Sāyaṇa then begins to cite the Anukramaṇī.

### Printed p. 120

> आ वोऽष्टौ सर्प ऐरावतो जरत्कर्णो ग्राव्णोऽस्तादिति॥ ग्रावस्तोत्र एतत्तूक्तं ।
> सूत्रितं च । आ व ऋंजसे प्र वो ग्रावाण इति सूक्तयोरंतरोपरिष्पात्पुरस्ताद्वा ।
> आ. ५-१२ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Anukramaṇī records the eight Ṛks beginning आ व (*ā va*) as of the serpent Jaratkarṇa Airāvata, addressed to the stones (grāvan). This sūkta is a praise of the stones. It is also laid down in the sūtra (Ā. 5-12) that the two sūktas आ व ऋंजसे (*ā va ṛṃjase*) and प्र वो ग्रावाण (*pra vo grāvāṇa*) are to be recited between, after or before. **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning आ वः (*ā vaḥ*) is the eighth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka; it has eight Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Jaratkarṇa, of the serpent kind, the son of Irāvata; its deity is the stones called ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*) that are used at the time of pressing Soma; the metre is Jagatī. In the Anukramaṇī it is said आवोष्टौ सर्प ऐरावतो जरत्कर्णो ग्राव्योऽसौत् (*āvoṣṭau sarpa airāvato jaratkarṇo grāvyo 'saut*). Therefore in this sūkta there is a praise of the stones. In the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra it is said आ व ऋंजसे प्र वो ग्रावाण इति सूक्तयोरंतरोपरिष्टात्सुरस्ताद्वा (*ā va ṛṃjase pra vo grāvāṇa iti sūktayoraṃtaropariṣṭātsurastādvā*) (Ā. 5.12).

Sūkta 76. Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 76. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 8, 9. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 8. Seer: जरत्कर्ण ऐरावतः सर्पः (*jaratkarṇa airāvataḥ sarpaḥ*). Deity: ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*). Metre: जगती (*jagatī*).

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ व॑ ऋंजस ऊ॒र्जां व्यु॑ष्टि॒स्विंद्रं॑ म॒रुतो॒ रोद॑सी अनक्तन ।  
> उ॒भे यथा॑ नो॒ अह॑नी सचा॒भुवा॒ सदः॑सदो वरिव॒स्त्यात॑  ॒॑
> उद्भिदा ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । वः । ऋंजसे । ऊर्जां । विउष्टिषु । इंद्रं । मरुतः । रोदसी इति । अनक्तन ।  
> उभे इति । यथा । नः । अहनी इति । सचाभुवा । सदःसदः । परिवस्यातः ।  
> उत्भिदा ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 121

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ग्रावाणः वो युष्मानूर्जां सारभूतानामन्नवतीनां पोषसां
> व्युष्टिषु विभातेषु सत्स्वा ऋंजसे । आप्रसाधयामि । यूयं सोमेनेंद्रं
> मरुतो रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ चानक्तन । व्यंजयत । नोऽस्मानुभे सचाभुवा
> सहोत्पन्ने अहनी द्यावापृथिव्यौ सदःसदः सर्वेषु यागगृहेषु वरिवस्यातः ।
> परिचरतः ॥ उद्भि दोद्भेदकेन धनेन पूरयतामिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Grāvans, I accomplish (worship) you at the dawnings of the dawns, which are the essence of food and rich in food. You manifest with Soma Indra, the Maruts, and heaven and earth. Both heaven and earth, born together, serve us in all the sacrificial houses. May they fill us with wealth that breaks forth abundantly. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—you (O Grāvas that press the Soma)
- ऊर्जां (*ūrjāṃ*)—of the dawns provided with food
- व्युष्टिषु (*vyuṣṭiṣu*)—at the time of manifestation
- आ यंजसे (*ā yaṃjase*)—I worship **NOTE**
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth
- अनक्तन (*anaktana*)—you illuminate
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सचाभुवा (*sacābhuvā*)—born together
- उभे अहनी (*ubhe ahanī*)—both heaven and earth
- सदःसदः (*sadaḥsadaḥ*)—in all the chambers of sacrifice
- वरिवस्मातः (*varivasmātaḥ*)—they attend **NOTE**
- उद्भिदा (*udbhidā*)—may they make us rich in possessions with abundant wealth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Grāvas that press the Soma, I worship you at the time of the manifestation of the dawns that possess food. You illuminate Indra, the Maruts, and heaven and earth. Heaven and earth, born together, attend on us in all the chambers of sacrifice. May they make us rich in possessions with abundant wealth.

##### English translation
I propitiate you at the commencement of the food-possessing (dawns); you reveal Indra, the Maruts, heaven and earth; both heaven and earth being united attend on us in all the chambers of sacrifice; (may they fill us) with wealth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this sūkta the Grāvas (stones), which are lifeless things, are conceived of as deities and praised. For Yāska's explanation of this matter and for the detailed description, see pages 100-102 of this same part.

When explaining the meaning of the word मुंजसे (*muṃjase*) in this Ṛk, Yāska says:

### Printed p. 122

ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मा । आ व ऋंजसे ऊर्जां व्युष्टिषु इत्यपि निगमो भवति । (*ṛṃjatiḥ prasādhanakarmā | ā va ṛṃjase ūrjāṃ vyuṣṭiṣu ityapi nigamo bhavati |*)

Thus he has given the etymology. ऋंजतिः (*ṛṃjatiḥ*) means "to adorn", that is, the idea is to praise especially by singing of the qualities. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तदु॒ श्रेष्ठं॒ सव॑नं सुनोत॒नात्यो॒ न हस्त॑यतो॒ अद्रिः॑  ॒॑
> सोतरि ।  
> वि॒दद्धग१्॒॑यो अ॒भिभू॑ति॒ पौंस्यं॑ म॒हो रा॒ये चि॑त्तरुते॒  ॑
> यदर्वतः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । ऊं इति । श्रेष्ठं । सवनं । सुनोतन । अत्यः । न । हस्तयतः । अद्रिः ।  
> सोतरि ।  
> विदत् । हि । अर्यः । अभिभूति । पौंस्यं । महः । राये । चित् । तरुते । यत् '  
> अर्वतः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ग्रावाणः यूयं तदु तमेव श्रेष्ठं प्रशस्यं सवनं सोमं सुनोतन ।
> अभिषुणुत । अथैकवदाह । अद्रिरभिषवग्रावा हस्तयतो हस्ताभ्यां गृहीतः सन्नत्यो
> न धृतोऽश्व इव भवति सोतर्यभिषवकर्तर्यध्वर्यौ । स यथा हस्ताभ्यां
> दृढगृहीतोऽपि बलवान् तद्वत् । अर्यो ग्राव्णामभिषवाय प्रेरको यजमानोऽभिभूति शत्रूणामभिभावुकं पौंस्यं बलं विदद्धि । लभते खलु देवेभ्यः ।
> यद्वा । अभिषवायाध्वय्दादेः प्रेरितो ग्रावाभिभूति पौंस्यं विदद्यजमानार्थं ।
> किंच महे महते राये चिद्दनायापि यद्यो ग्रावार्वतोऽश्वांस्तरुते तनुते
> प्रयच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Grāvans, press that very excellent, praiseworthy Soma. Then he speaks of one stone: the pressing stone, held in the two hands, becomes, in the presser, the adhvaryu who performs the pressing, like a restrained horse; just as that horse, though held firmly with the two hands, is strong, so is the stone. The sacrificer, who impels the Grāvans to press, obtains manly strength that overcomes enemies; he obtains it indeed from the gods. Or else: the stone, impelled by the adhvaryu and others for the pressing, obtains manly strength for the sacrificer. Further, for great wealth and also for giving, he who, as the stone, extends and bestows horses.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- श्रेष्ठं (*śreṣṭhaṃ*)—excellent (O Grāvas)
- तदु सवनं (*tadu savanaṃ*)—that very Soma
- सुनोतन (*sunotana*)—press
- सोतरि (*sotari*)—at the time when the adhvaryu presses the Soma
- अद्रिः (*adriḥ*)—the pressing stone

### Printed p. 123

- हस्तयितः (*hastayitaḥ*)—held by the hands
- अत्योन (*atyona*)—appears like a horse
- महे (*mahe*)—great
- राये चित् (*rāye cit*)—for wealth
- यत् (*yat*)—the stone which
- अर्वतः (*arvataḥ*)—horses
- तरुते (*tarute*)—provides (by that)
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—the sacrificer who is the impeller
- अभिभूति (*abhibhūti*)—able to defeat enemies
- पौंस्यं (*pauṃsyaṃ*)—strength
- विदद्धि (*vidaddhi*)—obtains

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Grāvas, press the excellent Soma. At the time when the adhvaryu presses the Soma, the pressing stone, held by the hands, appears like a horse. By the stone that provides horses for great wealth, the impelling sacrificer obtains strength able to defeat his enemies.

##### English translation
Pour forth this excellent libation; the stone grasped by the hand is like a horse when the grinder (effuses the Soma); the animating (worshipper) acquires virile strength overpowering (his foes) when (the stone) bestows horses for the sake of great wealth.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तदिद्द॑स्य॒ सव॑नं वि॒वेर॒पो यथा॑ पु॒रा मन॑वे गा॒तुमश्रे॑त् ।  
> गोअ॑र्णसि त्वा॒ष्टे अश्व॑निर्णिजि॒ प्रेम॑ध्व॒रेष्व॑ध्व॒रा अ॑तिश्रयुः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । इत् । हि । अस्य । सवनं । विवेः । अपः । यथा । पुरा । मनवे । गातुं । आश्रेत् ।  
> गोऽर्णसि । त्वाष्टे । अश्वनिर्निजि । प्र । ईं । अध्वरेषु । अध्वरान् । अशिश्रयुः॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदिति पूरणः । अस्य ग्राव्यस्तत्सवनमभिषवोपेशमस्मदीयं सोमयागाख्यमपः कर्म विवेः । व्याप्पोतु ॥ विपूर्वस्य वेतेर्लज्यदादित्वाच्छपो लुक् ।
> वेतेर्विपूर्वस्य वा छांदसे श्ला वापररूपमिति संदेहादनवग्रहः । समासस्वरः ॥
> यथा पुरा मनवे राजे गातुं गमनमश्रेत् आजगाम तद्वद्व्याप्नोतु । किंच

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The word इत् (*it*) is a filler. May that pressing of this stone-deity, our rite called the Soma sacrifice, pervade (विवेः (*viveḥ*)), just as it went formerly to Manu the king; that is, as गातुं (*gātuṃ*) (going) came to him, so may it pervade. Further:

### Printed p. 124

> गोअर्णसि गोरूपेऽपहृतैर्गोभिः परिवृते तथाश्वनिर्णिजि । निर्णिगिति रूपनाम ।
> अश्वरूपे । अपहृतैरश्वैर्वृत इत्यर्थः । ईदृशे त्वाष्टे त्वष्टुः पुत्रे हंतव्ये
> सतीति शेषः । ईमेतानध्वरानसुरैरहिंस्यानध्वरसाधकान्नोपलानध्वरेश्वशिश्रयुः ।
> आश्रयंति पूर्वे यजमानाः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When the son of Tvaṣṭṛ was to be slain, being surrounded by abducted cows in the form of a cow, and likewise अश्वनिर्णिजि (*aśvanirṇiji*), in the form of a horse (a word meaning form), that is, surrounded by abducted horses: at that time the former sacrificers resorted, in the sacrifices, to these stones, which the asuras cannot injure and which accomplish the sacrifice. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- पुरा (*purā*)—formerly
- मनवे (*manave*)—to Manu
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—going
- अश्रेत् (*aśret*)—it reached, in that manner
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this stone
- सवनं (*savanaṃ*)—in the form of the pressing of Soma
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the rite
- विवेः (*viveḥ*)—may it pervade
- गोआर्णसि (*goārṇasi*)—surrounded by abducted cows
- अश्वनिर्णिजि (*aśvanirṇiji*)—who has assumed the form of a horse
- त्वाष्टे (*tvāṣṭe*)—at the time when the son of Tvaṣṭṛ is slain
- ईं अध्वरान् (*īṃ adhvarān*)—these stones that accomplish the sacrifice
- अध्वरेषु (*adhvareṣu*)—in the sacrifices
- प्र आशिश्रयुः (*pra āśiśrayuḥ*)—the sacrificers took hold of

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As formerly its rite spread and went to Manu, so may this rite of this stone, in the form of the pressing of Soma, pervade. When the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, surrounded by abducted cows and having assumed the form of a horse, was to be slain, the sacrificers took hold, in the sacrifices, of these stones that accomplish the sacrifice, for the pressing of Soma.

##### English translation
May this (sacrificial) work of this grinding-stone, the effusion of the Soma, spread as it went formerly along the path to Manu: when the son of Twashtri, hidden by the (stolen) cows, and assuming the form of a horse, (was to be slain), the worshippers had recourse at the sacrifices to the inviolable (upper grinding-stones).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk there is mention of the slaying of the son of Tvaṣṭṛ by Indra. We have explained that matter in Part 14, pages 626-631.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अप॑ हत र॒क्षसो॑ भंगु॒राव॑तः स्कभा॒यत॒ निर्ऋ॑तिं॒ सेध॒ताम॑तिं।  
> आ नो॑ र॒यिं सर्व॑वीरं सुनोतन देवा॒व्यं॑ भरत॒ श्लोक॑मद्रयः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 125

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अप । हत । रक्षसः । भंगुरवतः । स्कभायत । निःऋतिं । सेधत । अमतिं ।  
> आ नः । रयिं । सर्ववीरं । सुनोतन । देव्यअव्यं । भरत । श्लोकं । अद्रयः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्रयः यूयमप हत विनाशयत रक्षसो राक्षसान् । कीदृशान् ।
> भंगुरावतो भंजकेन कर्मणा तद्वतः । तथा स्कभायत दूरे परिहरत निर्ऋतिं
> पापदेवतां । यथामुं यज्ञं नागच्छति तथा । किंच सेधत निषेधतामतिं
> हिंसामतिं रक्षः प्रकृतिं । किंच नोऽस्माकं सर्ववीरं बहुपुत्राद्युपेतं रयिं धनमा
> सुनोतन । अभिषवेन पुत्रधनादीनां लाभादेवमुक्तं । किंच देवाव्यं देवप्रीणनं
> श्लोकं भरत । संपादयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O stones, destroy the Rākṣasas, those who possess a rite that breaks (the sacrifice). Likewise drive far away निर्ऋतिं (*nirṛtiṃ*), the goddess of sin, so that she does not come to this sacrifice. Further, keep off अमतिं (*amatiṃ*), the intention to harm, the Rākṣasa nature. Further, bring forth for us wealth that has all heroes, that is, endowed with many sons and the like; this is said because sons, wealth and the like are obtained by pressing. Further, bring praise that gladdens the gods; procure it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आद्रयः (*ādrayaḥ*)—O stones
- भंगुरावतः (*bhaṃgurāvataḥ*)—who cause obstruction to the rite
- रक्षसः (*rakṣasaḥ*)—the Rākṣasas
- अप हत (*apa hata*)—destroy
- निर्ऋतिं (*nirṛtiṃ*)—the destructive goddess of sin
- स्कभायत (*skabhāyata*)—drive far away
- अनुतिं (*anutiṃ*)—those of evil intention **NOTE**
- सेधत (*sedhata*)—check
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- सर्ववीरं (*sarvavīraṃ*)—endowed with many sons and the like
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth
- आ सुनोतन (*ā sunotana*)—provide
- देवाव्यं (*devāvyaṃ*)—that gives satisfaction to the gods
- श्लोकं (*ślokaṃ*)—praise
- भरत (*bharata*)—procure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O stones, destroy the Rākṣasas who cause obstruction to the rite. Drive far away the destructive goddess of sin. Check those of evil intention. Provide for us wealth endowed with many sons and the like. Procure praise that gives satisfaction to the gods.

##### English translation
Drive away the disturbing Rakshasas; keep off Nirriti; prohibit all malignity; effuse for us riches with male progeny; bear, stones, the praise that delights the gods.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वश्चि॒दा वोऽम॑वत्तरेभ्यो वि॒भ्वना॑ चिदा॒श्व॑पस्तरेभ्यः ।  
> वा॒योश्चि॒दा सोम॑रभस्तरेभ्यो॒ऽग्नेश्चि॑तदर्च पितु॒कृत्त॑रेभ्यः ॥

### Printed p. 126

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवः । चित् । आ । वः । अमवत्तरेभ्यः । विभ्वना । चित् । अश्वपतरेभ्यः ।  
> वायोः । चित् । आ । सोमरभःतरेभ्यः । अग्नेः । चित् । अर्च । पितुकृत्तरेभ्यः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दिवश्चिदादित्यादप्यमवत्तरेभ्योऽत्यंतं बलवद्भो वो युष्मभ्यमार्च ।
> अस्तौ दध्वर्युः । यद्वा । हे अध्वय्यादयः वो यूयमुच्यमानलक्षणेभ्यो
> ग्रावभ्य आ सर्वतोऽर्च । अर्चतेत्येवं प्रतिवाक्यं योज्यं । विभ्वना चित् । विभ्वा
> सुधन्वनः पुत्रः । तेनाप्याश्वपस्तरेभ्यः । शीघ्रकर्मभ्य इत्यर्थः । विभ्वादीनां
> त्रयाणां चमसादिशीघ्रकर्म प्रसिद्धं । वायोश्चिद्वायोरपि सोमरभस्तरेभ्य।
> सोमाभिषवार्थेनात्यंतेन वेगेन युक्तेभ्यः । वायोरपि ग्रावाणो वेगवंतो
> भवंति सोमाभिषवार्थं । अग्नेश्चितगेरपि पितुकृत्तरेभ्योऽत्यंतमन्नसाधकेभ्यः :
> ईदृशेभ्यो ग्रावभ्यः प्रीणनाय यूयमर्चतेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

To you, O stones, who are exceedingly strong, stronger even than the sun (heaven), the adhvaryu offers praise. **NOTE** Or else: O adhvaryus and others, praise on all sides these stones, which have the characteristics now stated; the word "praise" is to be joined with every clause. Than Vibhvan also, who is the son of Sudhanvan, they are swifter in work, that is, quick in action; the quick work with the cup and the like of the three, Vibhvan and the others, is well known. Than Vāyu too they are swifter in pressing Soma, being endowed with exceeding speed for the pressing of Soma; the stones become speedier even than Vāyu for the pressing of Soma. Than Agni too they are exceedingly accomplishers of food. For the gratification of stones of such kind, praise them. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवश्चित् (*divaścit*)—than the sun
- अमवत्तरेभ्यः (*amavattarebhyaḥ*)—to those exceedingly strong
- विभ्वना चित् (*vibhvanā cit*)—than Vibhvan
- अश्वपस्तरेभ्यः (*aśvapastarebhyaḥ*)—to those who work swiftly
- वायोश्चित् (*vāyościt*)—than Vāyu
- सोमरभस्तरेभ्यः (*somarabhastarebhyaḥ*)—to those who press Soma more swiftly
- अग्नेश्चित् (*agneścit*)—more than Agni
- पितुकृत्तरेभ्यः (*pitukṛttarebhyaḥ*)—to those who are accomplishers of food
- व (*va*)—addressing you **NOTE**
- आ अर्च (*ā arca*)—the adhvaryu praises

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O stones, the adhvaryu praises you, who are stronger even than the sun, swifter in work than Vibhvan, swifter than Vāyu in pressing Soma, and more than Agni accomplishers of food.

##### English translation
(The Adhwaryu) praises you who are stronger even than heaven, quicker in work than Vibhwan, more diffusive of the Soma than Vayu, more bountiful of food than Agni.

### Printed p. 127

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भु॒रंतु॑ नो य॒शसः॒ सोत्त्वंध॑सो॒ ग्रावा॑णो वा॒चा दि॒विता॑  ॒॑
> दि॒वित्म॑ता ।  
> नरो॒ यत्र॑ दुह॒ते काम्यं॒ मध्वा॑घो॒षयं॑तो अ॒भितो॑  ॒॑
> मिथस्तुरः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भुरंतु । नः । यशसः । सोतु । अंधसः । ग्रावाणः । वाचा । दिविता । दिवित्मता ।  
> नरः । यत्र । दुहते । काम्यं । मधु । आघोषयंतः । अभितः । मिथस्तुरः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यशसो यशस्विनो ग्रावाणो नोऽस्मभ्यं सोतु सुतमंधसः सोमस्य
> रसं भुरंतु । भरंतु ! संपादयंतु ॥ उकारश्कांदसः ॥ किंच दिवित्मत्ता दीप्तिमत्या !!
> लिंगव्यत्ययः ॥ वाचा स्तुतिवाचा दिविता दिवितायां दीप्तिमत्तायां वास्मान्कुर्वंतु । सोमयागे स्थापयंतित्यर्थः । क्वैषा दीप्तिमत्तेति उच्यते । नरो
> नेतार ऋत्विजो यत्र यागे काम्यं कमनीयं मधु सोमरसं दुहते ।
> किं कुर्वंतः । अघोषयंतः सर्वतोऽभिषवशब्दं स्तोत्रशस्त्रादिरूपं ना शब्दं
> कुर्वंतः । अभितः सर्वतो मिथस्तुरस्वरमाणाः । सा दीप्तिमत्तेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May the renowned stones bring for us the pressed juice of Soma; भुरंतु (*bhuraṃtu*) means "may they bring, may they procure". Further, may they place us in brilliant praise-speech, that is, may they establish us in the Soma sacrifice endowed with brilliance. Where is this brilliance said to be? The leading priests, the officiants, where in the sacrifice they milk the desirable honey, the Soma juice. Doing what? Making the sound of pressing on all sides, in the form of stotra and śastra and so on, and hastening one another all around. That is the brilliance. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यशसः (*yaśasaḥ*)—endowed with fame
- ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*)—the stones
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- सोतु (*sotu*)—the pressed
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—Soma juice
- भुरंतु (*bhuraṃtu*)—may they procure
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the priests who lead the rite
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which sacrifice
- आघोषयंतः (*āghoṣayaṃtaḥ*)—making the sound of praise
- अभितः (*abhitaḥ*)—all around
- मिथस्तुरः (*mithasturaḥ*)—and being intent on the rite
- काम्यं (*kāmyaṃ*)—attractive
- मधु (*madhu*)—the Soma juice
- दुहते (*duhate*)—they milk, such
- दिविता (*divitā*)—in the radiant house of sacrifice
- दिवित्मता (*divitmatā*)—radiant
- वाचा (*vācā*)—may they make us endowed with a word of praise

### Printed p. 128

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the stones endowed with fame procure for us the pressed Soma juice. May they make us endowed with radiant praise-speech in that radiant house of sacrifice in which the priests who lead the rite, making the sound of praise and being everywhere intent on the rite, milk the attractive Soma juice.

##### English translation
May the renowned stones bring us the effused (juice) of the Soma, (may they establish us) with brilliant praise in the brilliant (Soma-sacrifice), where the priests milk forth the desirable Soma-juice, crying out all around, and racing each other.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒न्वंति॒ सोमं॑ रथि॒रासो॒ अद्र॑यो॒ निर॑स्य॒ रसं॑ ग॒विषो॑ ॒ 
> दुहंति ते ।  
> दु॒हंतूध॑रुप॒सेच॑नाय॒ कं नरो॑ ह॒व्या न म॑र्जयंत  ॒॑
> आसभिः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुन्वंति । सोमं । रथिरासः । अद्रयः । निः । अस्य । रसं । गोऽइषः । दुहंति । ते ।  
> दुहंति । ऊधः । उपसेचनाय । कं । नरः । हव्या । न । मर्जयंते 8 आसभिः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रथिरासो रथवंतो रंहणवंतोऽद्रयो ग्रावाणः सोमं सुन्वंति ।
> ग्रावाणोऽस्य सोमस्य रसं निर्दुहंति । निःशेषेण दुहंति गविषः स्तुतिवाचमिच्छंतः
> संतः । दुहंतूधा रसं सोमसंबंधिनं । किमर्थं । उपसेचनायाग्नेः । कमिति
> पादपूरणः । नरो नेतारोऽभिषवकर्तार ऋत्विजो हव्या हवींषि जाग्युपसेचनानंतरमासभीः स्वास्मैर्मर्जयंते। शोधयंति। शेषभक्षणेन शुद्धीकुर्वंति ।
> यद्वा । अग्नौ होतुर्दशापवित्रात्पुरासभिरास्योपलक्षितैः स्तोत्रैर्मर्जयंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The stones, endowed with speed, press Soma. The stones milk out completely the juice of this Soma, wishing, as they are, for the praise-speech. May they milk the juice belonging to Soma, for what purpose? For the sprinkling of the fire; कं (*kaṃ*) is a filler. The leading priests, the pressers and officiants, after the sprinkling purify the oblations with their mouths, cleanse them, making them pure by eating the remainder. Or else: in the fire, before the Hotṛ's ten-fold strainer, they purify with praises marked by the mouth. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 129

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रथिरासः (*rathirāsaḥ*)—swift-moving
- ते अद्रयः (*te adrayaḥ*)—those stones
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- सुन्वंति (*sunvaṃti*)—they press
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Soma
- रसं (*rasaṃ*)—the juice
- निः दुहंति (*niḥ duhaṃti*)—they milk completely
- गविषः (*gaviṣaḥ*)—desiring praises
- उपसेचनाय (*upasecanāya*)—for sprinkling on the fire
- ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)—the juice
- दुहंति (*duhaṃti*)—they milk
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the priests
- हव्या न (*havyā na*)—the oblations
- आसभिः (*āsabhiḥ*)—with their mouths
- मर्जयंते (*marjayaṃte*)—they purify

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The swift-moving stones press the Soma. They milk out completely the juice of this Soma. Desiring praises, they milk out the juice for sprinkling on the fire. The priests purify the oblations with their mouths (by the recitation of hymns).

##### English translation
The swiftly-moving grinding-stones effuse the Soma; desiring praise, they milk forth its juice; they milk forth the juice for the sprinkling (of Agni); the leaders (of rites) purify the oblations with their mouths.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ते न॑रः॒ स्वप॑सो अभूतन॒ य इंद्रा॑य सुनु॒थ सोम॑मद्रयः ।  
> वा॒मुंवा॑मं वो दि॒व्याय॒ धाम्ने॒ हसु॑वसु वः॒ पार्थि॑वाय  ॒
> सुन्वते ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एते । नरः । सुअपसः । अभूतन । ये । इंद्राय । सुनुथ । सोमं । अद्रयः ।  
> वामंवामु । वः । दिव्याय । धान्ने । वसुवसु । वः । पार्थिवाय । सुन्वते ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरो नेतारो हे अद्रयः एते यूयं स्वपसोऽभूतन । शोभनाभिसवकर्माणो भवत । ये यूयमिंद्राय सोमं सुनुथ वो यूयं
> xxix 9
> xxix 9

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders, O stones, be you those who do the pressing well, you who press Soma for Indra. Whatever

### Printed p. 130

> वामंवामं यद्यद्वननीयं धनमस्ति तत्तद्दिव्याय धान्ने तेजसे कुरुत । तथा
> वसुवसु यद्यद्वासयोग्यं धनमस्ति तद्वो यूयं पार्थिनाय सुन्वते
> यजमानाय कुरुत !!

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

desirable wealth there is, make that for divine splendour; and likewise whatever wealth is fit for dwelling, make that for the earthly sacrificer who presses.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरः (*naraḥ*)—leaders of the rite
- अद्रयः (*adrayaḥ*)—O stones
- एते (*ete*)—you
- स्वपसः अभूतन (*svapasaḥ abhūtana*)—you are those who do excellent work
- ये (*ye*)—you who
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- सुनुथ (*sunutha*)—you milk, such
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- वामंवामं (*vāmaṃvāmaṃ*)—very attractive wealth
- दिव्याय (*divyāya*)—of the world of the gods
- धान्ने (*dhānne*)—for splendour make it arise
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- पार्थिवाय (*pārthivāya*)—born on the earth
- सुन्वते (*sunvate*)—to the sacrificer who presses Soma
- वसुवसु (*vasuvasu*)—give all wealth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O stones, leaders of the rite, you are excellent leaders of the rite. You who milk Soma for Indra, make very attractive wealth arise for the splendour of the world of the gods. Give all wealth to the sacrificer who presses Soma on the earth.

##### English translation
You stones, leaders (of rites), you are the doers of good work; you who express the Soma for Indra, (make) all desirable wealth for the glory of heaven; you (give) all treasures to the earthly effuser.

## Sūkta 77 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अभ्रपुष इत्यष्टर्चं नवमं सूक्तं भृगुगोत्रस्य स्यूमरश्मेरार्षं
> मरुद्देवताकं । पंचमी जगती शिष्टाः सप्त त्रिष्टुभः । तथा चानुक्रांतं । अभ्रपुषः
> स्युमरश्मिर्भार्गवो मारुतं तु पंचमी जगतीति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ninth sūkta, beginning अभ्रपुष (*abhrapuṣa*), has eight Ṛks. Its seer is Syūmaraśmi of the Bhṛgu lineage; its deity is the Maruts. The fifth Ṛk is in Jagatī, the remaining seven are in Triṣṭubh. So the Anukramaṇī records: the sūkta अभ्रपुषः (*abhrapuṣaḥ*), Syūmaraśmi Bhārgava, addressed to the Maruts, the fifth Ṛk being Jagatī. The ritual application has already been given.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning अभ्रपुषः (*abhrapuṣaḥ*) is the ninth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has eight Ṛks. Its seer is Syūmaraśmi of the Bhṛgu lineage; the Maruts are the deity; the fifth Ṛk of this sūkta is in the Jagatī metre; the remaining seven Ṛks are in the Triṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said अभ्रपुषः स्यूमरश्मिर्भार्गवो मारुतं तु पंचमी जगती (*abhrapuṣaḥ syūmaraśmirbhārgavo mārutaṃ tu paṃcamī jagatī*). The ritual application has been stated earlier.

### Printed p. 131

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 77. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 10, 11. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 8. Seer: स्युमरश्मिर्भार्गवः (*syumaraśmirbhārgavaḥ*). Deity: मरुतः (*marutaḥ*). Metre: Ṛks 1-4 and 6 onward Triṣṭubh; Ṛk 5 Jagatī. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भ्र॒पुषो॒ न वा॒चा पु॑षा॒ वसु॑ ह॒विष्मं॑तो॒ न य॒ज्ञा  ॒॑॑
> वि॑जा॒नुषः॑ ।  
> सु॒मारु॑तं॒ न ब्र॒ह्माण॑म॒र्हसे॑ ग॒णम॑स्तोष्येषां॒ न शो॒भसे॑॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभ्रपुषः । न । वाचा । पुषः । वसु । हविष्मंतः । न । यज्ञाः । विजानुषः ।  
> सुमारुतं । न । ब्रह्माणं । अर्हसे । गणं । अस्तोषि । एषां । न । शोभसे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अभ्रपुषो न मेघान्निर्गच्छंत उदकबिंदव इव वाचा स्तुत्या प्रीता
> मरुतो वसु धनं पुष । सिंचंति ॥ व्यत्ययेन बहुवचनं ॥ किंच हविष्मंतो न
> यॆज्ञा हविर्भियुक्ता यागा इव विजानुषो जगतो विजनयितारो भवंति । अथ
> समुदायेनाह । तेषामेषां सुमारुतं शोभमानानां मरुतां ब्रह्माणं
> महांतं गणमर्हसे पूजार्थं नास्तोषि । नास्तोषं । न स्तुतवानस्मितः पूर्वं ।
> तथा शोभसे शोभार्थमपि मारुतं गणं नास्तौषि । अत इदानीं नूतनेन
> स्तोत्रेण सौमात्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Maruts, pleased with praise, shower wealth like drops of water coming out of the clouds (the plural verb is used by a change of number). Further, like sacrifices endowed with oblations, they become the generators of the world. Then he speaks of the whole company: I did not formerly praise the great band of these shining Maruts for worship, nor, likewise, did I praise the band of the Maruts for glory; therefore now with a new hymn. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाचा (*vācā*)—the Maruts, pleased by our praise
- अभ्रपुषो न (*abhrapuṣo na*)—like the rain that a cloud pours
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- पुष (*puṣa*)—they pour
- हविष्मंतः (*haviṣmaṃtaḥ*)—filled with oblations
- यज्ञाः न (*yajñāḥ na*)—like sacrifices
- विजानुषः (*vijānuṣaḥ*)—they become producers for the world
- एषां सुमारुतं (*eṣāṃ sumārutaṃ*)—of the excellent Maruts

### Printed p. 132

- ब्रह्माणं (*brahmāṇaṃ*)—endowed with greatness
- गणं (*gaṇaṃ*)—the host
- अर्हसे (*arhase*)—in accordance with their worthiness
- न अस्तोषि (*na astoṣi*)—I have not praised
- शोभसे (*śobhase*)—in keeping with their dignity
- न (*na*)—I have not praised

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Maruts, pleased with our praise, shower wealth like rain that a cloud pours. Like sacrifices filled with oblations, they become producers for the world. I have not praised the host of the excellent Maruts, endowed with greatness, in accordance with their worthiness. I have not praised them in keeping with their dignity.

##### English translation
(The Maruts), pleased with our praise like waterdrops sent down by clouds, shower wealth; they are the generators (of the world) like sacrifices abounding with oblations ; I have not praised the mighty band of Maruts (adequately) for their honour or for their glory.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्रि॒ये मर्या॑सो अं॒जीर॑कृण्वत सु॒मारु॑तं॒ न पू॒र्वीरति॒  ॑
> क्षपः ।  
> दि॒वस्पु॒त्रास॒ एता॒ न ये॑तिर आदि॒त्यास॒स्ते अ॒क्रा न वा॑वृधुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्रिये । मर्यासः । अंजीन् । अकृण्वत । सुमारुतं । न । पूर्वीः । अति । क्षपः ।  
> दिवः । पुत्रासः । एताः । न । येतिरे । आदित्यासः । ते । अक्राः । न । ववृधुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> श्रिये शोभार्थं मर्यासो मारका मनुष्यरूपा ना मरुतः । पूर्वं
> मनुष्याः संतः पश्चात्सुकृतविशेषेण ह्यमरा आसन् । तेंजीनंजकान्याभरणान्यकृण्वत। कुर्वंति । किमर्थं। श्रिये स्वशरीरशोभनार्थं । सुमारुतं शोभमानानां
> मरुतां गणं पूर्वीर्बह्वः क्षपः क्षपयित्र्यः सेना नाति । न पराभावयंतीति

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Maruts, the slayers, in human form, for splendour, that is, for the beauty of their own bodies, make ornaments (अंजीन् (*aṃjīn*)), adorning themselves. They were formerly men, and afterwards, by special merit, became immortal. Many destroying armies do not overcome the shining band of the Maruts; this is what is to be supplied.

### Printed p. 133

> शेषः । किंच दिवो द्युदेवतायाः पुत्रासः पुत्रा एता गंतारो न येतिरे !
> निर्गच्छंति । त आदित्यासोऽदितेः पुत्रा अक्रा आक्रमणशीला मरुतो न वावृधुः ।
> न वर्धं ९ । यतोऽस्माभिर्न स्तुता आत इति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Further, the sons of heaven, the deity of the sky, do not go out, that is, do not set forth; those Ādityas, the sons of Aditi, the aggressive Maruts, did not grow, because they were not praised by us; this is the meaning. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मर्यासः (*maryāsaḥ*)—the Maruts in human form
- अंजीन् (*aṃjīn*)—ornaments
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—for the decoration of their bodies
- अकृण्वत (*akṛṇvata*)—they make for themselves
- सुमारुतं (*sumārutaṃ*)—the band of the excellent Maruts
- पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*)—very numerous
- क्षपः (*kṣapaḥ*)—an army
- न अति (*na ati*)—cannot defeat
- दिवः पुत्रासः (*divaḥ putrāsaḥ*)—sons of the world of heaven
- एताः (*etāḥ*)—these Maruts
- न येतिरे (*na yetire*)—do not set out (without our praise)
- आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*)—sons of Aditi
- अक्राः (*akrāḥ*)—pervasive
- ते (*te*)—they
- न वावृधुः (*na vāvṛdhuḥ*)—do not even gain strength (without our praise)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Maruts in human form make ornaments for the decoration of their bodies. Even a very numerous army cannot defeat the band of the excellent Maruts. These Maruts, the sons of the world of heaven, do not set out without our praise. These sons of Aditi, who are pervasive, do not even gain strength without our praise.

##### English translation
The destructive (Maruts) made ornaments for their decoration: many hostile hosts cannot overcome the band of Maruts; the moving sons of heaven do not issue forth, the swift-going sons of Aditi do not increase (in glory).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ये दि॒वः पृ॑थि॒व्या न ब॒र्हणा॒ त्मना॑ रिरि॒चे अ॒भ्रान्न सूर्यः॑।  
> पाज॑स्वंतो॒ न वी॒राः प॑न॒स्यवो॑ रि॒शाद॑सो॒ न मर्या॑ अ॒भिद्य॑वः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ये । दिवः । पृथिव्याः । न । बर्हणा । त्मना । रिरिचे । अभ्रात् । न । सूर्यः ।  
> पाजस्वंतः । न । वीराः ! पनस्यवः । रिशादसः । न । मर्याः । अभिद्यव ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 134

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ये मरुतो दिवः पृथिव्या न पृथिव्याश्च । नेति चार्थे । बर्हणा महत्वेन
> त्तनात्मनैव रिरिचे अतिरिक्ता अभवन्स्वशरीरेण अभ्रान्नाभ्रादिव सूर्यः । किंच
> पाजस्वंतो न वीरा बलवंतो वीरा इव पनस्यवः स्तुतिकामा भवंति । किंच रिशादसो
> न मर्या रिशतामसितारो मनुष्या इवाभिद्यवोऽभिगतदीप्तयो भवंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Maruts who, by greatness, from heaven and from earth, by their own selves became surpassing, in their own bodies, like the sun from a cloud. Further, like mighty heroes they become desirous of praise. Further, like men who cast down their enemies, they become radiant. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अभ्रात् (*abhrāt*)—coming out of the cloud
- सूर्यः न (*sūryaḥ na*)—like the sun
- ये (*ye*)—which Maruts
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the world of heaven
- पृथिव्याः न (*pṛthivyāḥ na*)—and from the earth
- बर्हणा (*barhaṇā*)—possessing greatness
- त्मना (*tmanā*)—and self-impelled
- प्र रिरिच्रे (*pra riricre*)—who have become manifest
- पाजस्वंतः (*pājasvaṃtaḥ*)—endowed with strength
- वीराः न (*vīrāḥ na*)—like heroes
- पनस्यवः (*panasyavaḥ*)—they become desirous of praise
- रिशादसः (*riśādasaḥ*)—destroyers of enemies
- मर्याः न (*maryāḥ na*)—like heroic men
- अभिद्यवः (*abhidyavaḥ*)—they become radiant

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those Maruts who, possessing greatness and self-impelled, become manifest from the world of heaven and from the earth, as the sun comes out shining from a cloud, desire praise like heroes endowed with strength. They become radiant like heroic men who destroy their enemies.

##### English translation
(The Maruts), who through the might of heaven and earth have emerged from their own persons as the sun emerges from the cloud, (are) desirous of praise like powerful heroes, and radiant like men who overpower their enemies.

#### Ṛk 4
